# BT17 audit

## Status

All 102 BT17 cards carry current, independently reproduced 10/10 evidence: 1020/1020 points on the 2026-09-09 re-audit ledger. The re-audit did not inherit the 2026-09-02 completion claims in `docs/audits/BT17-AUDIT.md`; every row started at zero and earned credit only from a report in this document. Where the older reports disagree with the re-audit, the re-audit wins because it is the newest dated ledger and the only one whose commands were rerun on the current tree. Eight engine seams were closed during the run and are recorded under Mechanisms.

## Gates

Commands and results below are copied from the winning report, the 2026-09-09 re-audit. The run recorded its own logs under `docs/audits/BT17-reaudit/logs/`; those files are not in the tree at `eabe99351` and the commands are the record.

#### Initial inspection

102 catalog cards, 102 direct modules, 102 colocated tests plus 5 range/audit
files and the catalog-sync test. No BT17 module calls `registerCard`. No
`@ts-nocheck`. KB: 254 Q&A references over 79 cards, 0 errata, 1 banlist entry
(BT17-069). Smells: 37 of 109 test files use injected timing
(`advance(...)`/`fireTiming`/`fireSubTrigger`); Digi-Egg `BT1-001` sits in
security in BT17-034, 037, 056, 059; 17 numeric `security: <n>` fixtures.

#### Session 1 checkpoint (start)

Baseline: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17 --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
=> 109 files, 675 tests passed in 10.43s (logs/baseline-collection.log).

## Card ledger

Merged from `docs/audits/BT17-reaudit/BT17-<NNN>.md` (102 reports, last commit 55dc1f2b1).

### BT17-001 — Gigimon

#### Card

Digi-Egg, Lv2, Red, Lesser. No main effect.
Inherited: `[When Attacking] By paying 1 cost, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 3000 DP or less.`
Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`) matches the official text. No discrepancy.

#### Q&A

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-001` returns no knowledge-base entries. KB-INDEX lists none.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[When Attacking]` inherited | `pays 1 memory and deletes ...` (real `attack` intent from the host carrying BT17-001 under BT17-007) | `trigger: "WhenAttacking"`, `isInherited: true` |
| `By paying 1 cost` | same test: memory 2 -> 1 | `cost: { kind: "payMemory", memory: 1 }` |
| `delete 1 ... 3000 DP or less` | same test deletes BT1-009 (exactly 3000 DP) and leaves the battle target alive | `Delete` with `dp: { op: "lte", value: 3000 }`, `count: 1` |
| No legal target | `does not pay memory or delete ...` (BT1-014, 4000 DP): memory stays 2, no trash | `condition: opponentHas` guard |
| IR shape | `exports the inherited paid deletion contract` | whole effect |

The boundary is covered on both sides: 3000 DP deleted, 4000 DP untouched, and the negative case proves the cost is not paid when the effect cannot resolve.

#### Peer/stack proof (added)

`carries the inherited deletion through the real Digi-Egg route: hatch -> digivolve -> battle area`
walks the whole Digi-Egg lifecycle with public intents only, under the production turn loop:

1. `hatchEgg` in the real Breeding window moves BT17-001 off the egg deck (egg deck empty, breeding occupied).
2. `digivolve` puts the Red Lv.3 BT17-007 onto it inside the breeding area (Lv.2 Red, cost 0); the egg instance is asserted as the whole stack and the battle area is still empty.
3. On the next own turn `moveFromBreeding` carries the stack into the battle area; the carrier's top card is BT17-007 and its stack is exactly the hatched egg instance.
4. Only then does the inherited `[When Attacking]` clause fire from under the real host: memory 2 -> 1 and the opposing 3000 DP peer (BT1-009) is trashed, while the 4000 DP peer (BT1-014) beside it survives — the DP boundary asserted with two peers on the board at once.

The turn loop is closed with a `surrender` intent and awaited, and no decision is left pending.

#### Commands

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-001.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 4 passed.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` — clean.
- `pnpm exec oxlint <files>`, `pnpm exec oxfmt --check <files>`, `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Changes

None. Module and test already met the standard; no injected timing, no Digi-Egg in security or deck, no numeric `security: <n>`.

#### Gaps

- No test that the egg does nothing while it is the top card of a breeding permanent or sitting in the deck; the inherited-only nature is argued from `isInherited`, not proven behaviourally.
- The `opponentHas` condition is an implementation guard, not printed text. It is correct (a cost may not be paid without a legal effect) but no ruling is cited for it.

#### Score (worker rubric, gates 0)

catalog/rules 2, IR trace 2, behavioural 2, peer/stack 2, delivery gates 0 — **8/10**.

### BT17-002 — Xiaomon

#### Card

Digi-Egg, Lv2, Blue, Lesser. No main effect.
Inherited: `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When one of your Digimon is played from digivolution cards, ＜Draw 1＞.`
Catalog matches the official text. No discrepancy.

#### Q&A

Q2702 (2024-03-28): two Digimon played from digivolution cards at the same time still draw only once — they are played in a single timing.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Your Turn]` | `does not draw on the opponent's turn` (turnSeat 1, deck and hand unchanged) | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| `When one of your Digimon is played from digivolution cards` | `Q2702: draws only once ...` and `does not draw when a Digimon is played from the hand` | `SubTrigger` `whenPlayed` with `sourceFilter { controller: "mine", kind: ["Digimon"], fromDigivolution: true }` |
| `＜Draw 1＞` | hand length 1 after the stack play | `Draw { controller: "mine", amount: 1 }` |
| `[Once Per Turn]`, same-turn refusal | `does not draw a second time for a later stack play in the same turn` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| `[Once Per Turn]`, next-own-turn reset | **added** `draws again on the next own turn after the once-per-turn use is spent` | same |
| Q2702 simultaneity | `Q2702: ...` plays both instances in one `playInstances` call: 3 permanents, one draw, stack left holding only BT17-002 | `SubTrigger` fires once per timing |
| IR shape | `exports the once-per-turn inherited digivolution-play watcher` | whole effect |

The added test spends the use, refuses the second stack play in the same turn, then runs a real seat-0 turn and a real seat-1 turn through `advance().runTurn` (with the hand-laid `turnSeat`/memory chaining the harness requires) and proves the third stack play draws again.

#### Peer/stack proof (added)

`carries the inherited draw through the real Digi-Egg route: hatch -> digivolve -> battle area`
walks the whole Digi-Egg lifecycle with public intents only, under the production turn loop:
`hatchEgg` -> `digivolve` the Blue Lv.3 BT1-028 onto the egg inside the breeding area (Lv.2 Blue,
cost 0, egg asserted as the stack) -> `moveFromBreeding` on the next own turn (carrier top card
BT1-028, stack exactly the hatched egg instance).

The comparative case is the card-kind boundary, taken on the same carrier in the same turn: a
Tamer (BT1-085) played out of the carrier's digivolution cards draws nothing, and a Digimon
(BT1-010) played out of the same digivolution cards draws exactly one. Note the printed text is
"one of your Digimon", not "this Digimon's", so a peer permanent WITHOUT BT17-002 beneath is not a
near miss — a stack play anywhere on your board legitimately draws. That was verified while
building this test and is why the kind boundary is the comparative used.

#### Commands

### BT17-003 — Bibimon

#### Card

Digi-Egg, Lv2, Yellow, Lesser / Abadin Electronics. No main effect.
Inherited: `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When an effect places a Tamer card in this Digimon's digivolution cards, gain 1 memory.`
Catalog matches the official text. No discrepancy.

#### Q&A

Q2703 (2024-03-28): activating ＜Mind Link＞ on a digivolution card of a Digimon carrying this card, placing a Tamer card, does gain 1 memory.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Your Turn]` | `does not gain memory on the opponent's turn` (turnSeat 1, memory unchanged, Tamer still placed) | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| `When an effect places ... in this Digimon's digivolution cards` | `Q2703: gains memory only when an effect places a Tamer in this inherited host` — a placement on a different host gains nothing | `SubTrigger` `onAddDigivolutionCards` with `triggerFilter { isSelfRef: true }` |
| `a Tamer card` | same test: placing a Digimon (BT1-010) gains nothing, placing BT1-085 gains 1 | `addedDigivolutionCardFilter { kind: ["Tamer"] }` |
| `gain 1 memory` | memory 0 -> 1 | `GainMemory { amount: 1 }` |
| `[Once Per Turn]`, same-turn refusal | `gains memory only once per turn across separate Tamer placements` (both cards land, memory 1) | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| `[Once Per Turn]`, next-own-turn reset | **added** `gains memory again on the next own turn after the once-per-turn use is spent` | same |
| IR shape | `exports the once-per-turn inherited Tamer-placement watcher` | whole effect |

#### Peer/stack proof (added)

`carries the inherited memory gain through the real Digi-Egg route: hatch -> digivolve -> battle area`
walks the whole Digi-Egg lifecycle with public intents only, under the production turn loop:
`hatchEgg` -> `digivolve` the Yellow Lv.3 BT1-045 onto the egg inside the breeding area (Lv.2
Yellow, cost 0, egg asserted as the stack) -> `moveFromBreeding` on the next own turn (carrier top
card BT1-045, stack exactly the hatched egg instance).

The comparative case runs on the same turn with both permanents on the board: a Tamer placed under
the near-miss peer (BT1-013, no BT17-003 beneath) leaves memory at 1, and the same placement under
the routed carrier takes it to 2, with the carrier's stack asserted as `["BT1-085", "BT17-003"]`.

#### Commands

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-003.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 6 passed.
- `tsc --noEmit`, `oxlint`, `oxfmt --check`, `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Changes

`apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-003.test.ts`: added the once-per-turn reset test. IR untouched, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is unaffected.

#### Gaps

- Q2703 is covered by the general shape of the ruling (an effect placing a Tamer into this host's digivolution cards gains memory), driven with `advance().verb.placeUnder`. The literal ＜Mind Link＞ route is not exercised, so the ruling's specific interaction is proven by analogy, not by the named keyword.
- No test that a Tamer placed as a cost by the controller's own card, or by an opponent's effect, behaves the same; the printed text does not restrict the effect's owner, so both should trigger.

#### Score (worker rubric, gates 0)

catalog/rules 2, IR trace 2, behavioural 2, peer/stack 2, delivery gates 0 — **8/10**.

### BT17-004 — Argomon

#### Card summary

Digi-Egg, Lv.2, Green, In-Training, Mutant, play cost -1, 0 DP, no evolution costs.

Printed clauses:

1. Main effect: none (`effectText` absent in the catalog).
2. Inherited: `[Opponent's Turn] While this Digimon is [Argomon], it gains ＜Blocker＞.`

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-004` returns "(no knowledge-base entries)", matching
`KB-INDEX.md` (0 Q&A, no errata, not on the banlist). Nothing to cover.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, no main effect | `compiled.effects` has exactly one entry | "matches every catalog field and carries its only printed clause in IR" |
| `[Opponent's Turn]` window | `trigger: "OpponentsTurn"` | "grants Blocker to an [Argomon] host only during the opponent's turn" (asserted absent on own turn, present on opponent's turn, absent again after the turn flips back) |
| `While this Digimon is [Argomon]` | `while: { kind: "selfHasName", names: ["Argomon"] }` | "does not grant Blocker to a non-[Argomon] host during the opponent's turn" (BT17-043 Terriermon host) |
| `it gains ＜Blocker＞` (inherited only) | `isInherited: true`, `Aura` + `keyword: Blocker`, `target.isSelfRef` | "lets the [Argomon] host actually block an opponent's player attack" and its negative twin |

Behavioural proof is a real block, not just a keyword read: seat 1 declares a player
attack, `blockWindowOpened` is observed, seat 0 answers with the public `declareBlock`
intent, and the endpoints are asserted after the attack resolves — seat 0's security is
still `["BT1-009", "BT1-013"]` in order, the 20000 DP blocker survives, the 5000 DP
attacker is gone from the battle area, and `state.pendingDecision` is undefined. The
negative run uses the same board with a Terriermon host: `declareBlock` is rejected
(`ok: false`) and the attack goes through to security, leaving `["BT1-013"]`.

#### Peer/stack proof (added)

`carries ＜Blocker＞ through the real Digi-Egg route beside a non-[Argomon] peer stack`
walks the whole Digi-Egg lifecycle with public intents only, under the production turn loop:
`hatchEgg` -> `digivolve` the Green Lv.3 [Argomon] BT17-042 onto the egg inside the breeding area
(egg asserted as the stack) -> `moveFromBreeding` on the next own turn.

The comparative case stands beside it for the whole test: a BT17-043 Terriermon carrying the same
BT17-004 Digi-Egg — same colour, same egg, wrong name. On the controller's own turn neither has
＜Blocker＞; on the opponent's turn only the routed [Argomon] carrier gains it. The name boundary is
therefore proven with both stacks live on the same board, not only across separate fixtures.

#### Commands and results

```
  => 1 file, 5 tests passed

pnpm exec oxlint <file>; pnpm exec oxfmt --check <file>  => clean
git diff --check -- <file>                               => clean
```

#### Defects fixed locally

- The previous suite had no catalog assertion and no IR equality: it only used
  `toMatchObject` on the first effect, so a wrong `Aura` target or a missing
  `isInherited` would have passed. Now `compiled.effects` is asserted with `toEqual`
  and every catalog field is pinned.
- The previous suite proved Blocker only through `observe(...).hasKeyword`, a
  ledger read. A granted keyword that the combat system never consults would have
  scored the same. The block is now exercised end to end through public intents.
- The previous "only on the opponent's turn" test never asserted the grant
  disappearing again when the turn returns to the controller.

#### Retained reds / seams

None.

#### Remaining gaps

- No interaction with a second Blocker source or with an effect that grants the
  [Argomon] name mid-turn.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `effectText` is absent, matching the official card's blank effect box.

| Peer/stack proof | 2 | Full hatch -> breeding digivolve -> `moveFromBreeding` route through public intents, with a BT17-043 Terriermon peer carrying the same Digi-Egg on the same board: only the [Argomon] stack gains ＜Blocker＞. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### BT17-005 — Tsumemon

#### Card summary

Digi-Egg, Lv.2, Black, In-Training, Unidentified, play cost -1, 0 DP, no evolution costs.

Printed clauses:

1. Main effect: none (`effectText` absent in the catalog).
2. Inherited: `[On Deletion] If this Digimon had the [Unidentified] trait, gain 1 memory.`
   (the catalog stores a non-breaking space between `[Unidentified]` and `trait`).

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-005` returns "(no knowledge-base entries)", matching
`KB-INDEX.md` (0 Q&A, no errata, not on the banlist). Nothing to cover.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, no main effect | `compiled.effects` has exactly one entry | "matches every catalog field and carries its only printed clause in IR" |
| `[On Deletion]`, inherited | `trigger: "OnDeletion"`, `isInherited: true` | "gains 1 memory when its [Unidentified] host loses a real battle" |
| `If this Digimon had the [Unidentified] trait` | `condition: { kind: "selfHasTrait", nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Unidentified"], match: "trait" }] }` | "does not gain memory when the deleted host lacks the [Unidentified] trait" (BT17-052 Agumon, Reptile/SoC) |
| `gain 1 memory` | `GainMemory { amount: 1 }` | positive test asserts `state.memory` 0 -> 1 |

Deletion is real, not injected. The host attacks a suspended 5000 DP opposing Digimon
and loses the battle, so `OnDeletion` fires from production combat resolution. Endpoints
asserted after the attack settles: the host permanent is gone from the battle area, both
`BT17-053` and `BT17-005` are in the trash, memory is exactly 1, and
`state.pendingDecision` is undefined. A third test attacks with the same
[Unidentified] host at 20000 DP: it survives, and memory stays 0 — the trigger is tied
to deletion, not to the trait alone.

The [Unidentified] host is BT17-053 Keramon, whose own printed clause only watches the
opponent playing a level 5 or higher Digimon, so it contributes nothing here.

#### Peer/stack proof (added)

`carries the inherited memory gain through the real Digi-Egg route beside a non-[Unidentified] peer stack`
walks the whole Digi-Egg lifecycle with public intents only, under the production turn loop:
`hatchEgg` -> `digivolve` the Black Lv.3 [Unidentified] BT17-053 onto the egg inside the breeding
area (Lv.2 Black, cost 0, egg asserted as the stack) -> `moveFromBreeding` on the next own turn.

The comparative case stands beside it: BT17-052 Agumon carrying the same BT17-005 Digi-Egg — same
colour, same egg, [Reptile]/[SoC] instead of [Unidentified]. Both attack the same 5000 DP wall in
the same turn and both die; the peer's deletion leaves memory at 3 and the routed [Unidentified]
carrier's deletion takes it to 4, with the hatched egg instance asserted in the trash.


#### Defects fixed locally

- The previous suite deleted the host with `advance(engine).verb.deletePermanent`, an
  injected verb. Under the re-audit standard that is structural plumbing, not
  behavioural proof; deletion now comes from a lost battle declared through the public
  `attack` intent.
- The previous positive fixture stacked `["BT17-005", "BT17-052", "BT17-054", "BT17-055"]`
  under a Lv.6 Diaboromon for no stated reason, mixing in three unrelated inherited
  effects. The fixture is now the minimum that proves the clause.
- No catalog assertion and no IR equality existed. Both were added; the
  `inheritedEffectText` assertion had to carry the catalog's U+00A0.
- No "survives" control existed, so nothing distinguished the trait condition from the
  deletion trigger.

#### Retained reds / seams

None.

#### Remaining gaps

- Memory is asserted as an absolute (`0 -> 1`) on a hand-laid board without the turn
  loop, so the pass-turn interaction of gaining memory is out of scope here.
- No multi-source deletion ordering (two On Deletion effects resolving together).

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `effectText` is absent, matching the official card's blank effect box. The
non-breaking space inside `inheritedEffectText` is a catalog formatting artefact
present across the set, not a text error.

| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### BT17-006 — Bowmon

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): DigiEgg, Lv2, Purple,
types `Lesser / X Antibody / SoC`, form `In-Training`. No main effect.

Printed clauses (inherited only):

1. `[Your Turn]` — the effect works only on its controller's turn.
2. `[Once Per Turn]` — one use per turn.
3. Trigger: "When an effect places a Tamer card in this Digimon's digivolution
   cards".
4. Effect: "this Digimon **may** digivolve into a Digimon card with the `[SoC]`
   trait in your trash" — optional, normal digivolution cost paid, requirements
   not waived (Q2705).

No catalog discrepancy found; the catalog text matches the official list.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Ruling | Where covered |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2704 | An effect placement (including `<Mind Link>`) that puts a Tamer under a Digimon with this egg does trigger the digivolve. | `Q2704/Q2705: digivolves the legal host into a SoC card from trash after an effect places a Tamer` — drives the production effect-placement seam (`advance().verb.placeUnder`), the same seam `<Mind Link>` uses. See "Remaining gaps" for why a literal `<Mind Link>` activation could not be added. |
| Q2705 | Digivolution requirements are **not** waived. | Positive: the Lv3 host pays 3 to reach the Lv4 `[SoC]` card. Negative: `Q2705: does not offer a SoC card whose digivolution requirement the host cannot meet` (Lv5 `[SoC]` from a Lv3 host is refused). |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Your Turn]` | `[Your Turn]: stays silent when the Tamer is placed during the opponent's turn` | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| `[Once Per Turn]` | `[Once Per Turn]: refuses a second placement this turn and resets on the next own turn` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Tamer placed in **this** Digimon's digivolution cards | `does not react when a Digimon, rather than a Tamer, is placed under the host`; `does not react when a Tamer is placed under a different host` | `SubTrigger { event: "onAddDigivolutionCards", triggerFilter: { isSelfRef: true }, addedDigivolutionCardFilter: { kind: ["Tamer"] } }` |
| may digivolve into a `[SoC]` Digimon card in your trash | `Q2704/Q2705…`; `picks the SoC card over an otherwise legal non-SoC card of the same level in the same trash` | `Digivolve { from: ["trash"], into: { kind: ["Digimon"], nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["SoC"], match: "trait" }] }, payCost: true, optional: true }` |
| requirements/cost respected | `Q2705: does not offer a SoC card…` (Lv5 refused, memory untouched) | `payCost: true` |

Structural check on the compiled IR is retained as the first test. The module
holds exactly one `registerIrCard("BT17-006", compiled)` and no `registerCard`;
`coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` — both honest, every printed clause is
implemented.

##### Peer / comparative and stack evidence

- Comparative case in one fixture: trash holds `BT4-080` Bakemon (Lv4 Purple,
  a legal route from the Lv3 Purple host, but **no** `[SoC]` trait) alongside
  `BT17-065` DexDorugamon (Lv4 Purple `[SoC]`). Only the `[SoC]` card is taken;
  Bakemon is asserted to remain in the trash and the memory delta (5 → 2) proves
  the DexDorugamon route, not the cheaper Bakemon route, was used.
- Real evolution-stack case: the once-per-turn test carries the egg through two
  digivolutions and asserts the full stack identity afterwards
  (`BT1-085, BT1-085, BT1-085, BT17-006, BT17-062, BT17-065` beneath a
  `BT17-067` top card), showing placed Tamers sit directly beneath the top card
  while the egg and both digivolution sources stay at the bottom.
- Cross-host negative: a second host of the same card id in the same fixture
  proves `isSelfRef` scoping.

### BT17-007 — Agumon

Lane 28. Base ea2dd8e22. Module IR unchanged; only the test file was extended.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Red Digimon, Lv3, Rookie,
Vaccine, Reptile, play cost 3, DP 1000, evoCosts Red Lv2 / Blue Lv2 at 0 memory.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve][Koromon]: Cost 0` — alternate digivolution route by exact name.
2. `[Start of Your Main Phase] If you have a Tamer with [Tai Kamiya] in its name,
   return 1 card with [Garurumon]/[Greymon]/[Omnimon] in its name from your trash
   to the hand.`
3. Inherited: `[End of Your Turn] This Digimon and another of your Digimon may DNA
   digivolve into a Digimon card in the hand.`

Catalog text uses U+00A0 before both "in its name" occurrences; the catalog test
asserts the exact bytes.

#### Q&A ids

| Id | Covered by |
| --- | --- |
| Q2706 (2 Tai Tamers still return only 1) | `returns only 1 card even with 2 Tamers named Tai Kamiya, per Q2706` |
| Q2707 (inherited DNA resolves before opponent's turn) | `resolves the inherited DNA digivolve while the turn is still yours, per Q2707` (records `turnSeat` at the moment the merge lands) |
| Q2708 (cannot DNA into a card without [DNA Digivolution]) | `does not use the inherited effect for a hand Digimon without DNA Digivolution` (BT17-078 Omnimon in hand, no DNA requirement) |
| Q2709 (cannot DNA with materials outside the requirement) | `refuses a DNA digivolve whose specified materials are not the pair in play, per Q2708 and Q2709` (two red Lv4 against Silphymon's Red Lv4 + Yellow Lv4) |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

- Clause 1 (`[Digivolve][Koromon]: Cost 0`) -> `compiled.digivolutionRequirement`
  `[{ names: ["Koromon"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]`.
  - Alternate route: off-color Yellow Koromon (BT12-003) in breeding digivolves
    into BT17-007 with `useAlternateCost: true`; only the `[Koromon]` route can
    legalize that source. Asserts stack identity, hand emptied, memory 0.
  - Normal route: Red Lv2 Yokomon (BT1-001) without `useAlternateCost`.
  - Illegal source: Yellow Kyaromon (BT1-005) — not Koromon, not Red/Blue — is
    rejected on both the normal and the alternate intent, with breeding top card,
    hand and memory unchanged.
- Clause 2 -> `effects[0]` `StartOfYourMainPhase` / `Return` to `hand`, target
  filter `zone: "trash", controller: "mine"`, `nameOrTrait` tokens
  `["Garurumon","Greymon","Omnimon"]` with `match: "name"` (substring, correct for
  "in its name"), `condition.kind: "youHave"` on a Tamer with `["Tai Kamiya"]`
  `match: "name"`.
  - Positive with an exact endpoint: WarGreymon (BT17-015) returns, WarGrowlmon
    (BT17-013, no matching token) stays in trash.
  - Q2706: two Tai Kamiya Tamers (BT1-085, ST1-12) and two matching trash cards
    return exactly one.
  - Negative: no Tai Tamer -> nothing returned.
  - Negative: Tai Tamer present but no matching name in trash -> nothing returned.
- Clause 3 -> `effects[1]` `EndOfYourTurn`, `isInherited: true`, `DnaDigivolve`
  with `isSelfRef` + `excludeSelf` materials and
  `into.hasDnaDigivolutionRequirement: true, zone: "hand"`, `payCost: true`,
  `optional: true`.
  - Positive: BT17-010 (with BT17-007 in its digivolution cards) + BT1-051 merge
    into BT16-012 Silphymon; merged stack contains all four cards, hand loses the
    Silphymon.
  - Q2707 timing, Q2708 and Q2709 negatives as tabled above.

All behavioural tests run through the real turn loop (`advance(...).runTurn(0)`)
or public `applyIntent` digivolve intents. No injected timing
(`fire`, `fireTiming`, `fireSubTrigger`) is used anywhere in this file. Fixtures
carry no Digi-Egg in security or deck and no numeric `security: <n>` form.

#### Defects

including the substring `match: "name"` semantics for both name filters. The

covered the return clause with only one candidate and asserted "one returned"

- Q2707's "before it becomes your opponent's turn" is asserted by sampling
  `state.turnSeat` when the merged permanent first appears during the polling
  settle. That is the strongest public signal available; a dedicated end-of-turn
  window observer would be a firmer proof.
- The `[Koromon]` alternate route's cost 0 cannot be distinguished from the
  printed Red/Blue Lv2 cost 0 by memory alone, since both are 0. The off-color
  Yellow Koromon source isolates the route by legality instead.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Catalog text matches the official list, with U+00A0 separators noted above.

#### Peer/stack proof (session 3 addition)

Two comparative tests in `BT17-007.test.ts` isolate the substring name gate of the
[Start of Your Main Phase] condition:

- "counts a Tamer with [Tai Kamiya] inside a longer name but not a different Tamer" —
  BT17-081 "Tai Kamiya & Matt Ishida" sits beside the near-miss peer BT1-086 "Matt Ishida".
  The trashed WarGreymon returns, proving `match: "name"` (substring) is the right mode:
  an exact gate would refuse BT17-081, since `effectiveExactNames` does not split "&" names.
- "does not fire for a near-miss Tamer peer that has no [Tai Kamiya] in its name" —
  BT1-086 "Matt Ishida" and BT1-087 "T.K. Takaishi" together leave the trash untouched.

Both run through `advance().runTurn(0)`; neither peer card is imported, so only its catalog
identity is in play. Together with the DNA-merge stack tests, this closes the peer column.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog/rules | 2 | Catalog asserted field by field; all four Q&A ids read from the KB and mapped to tests. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every printed clause maps to a named IR node; `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` verified honest. |
| Behavioural | 2 | 13 behavioural tests through the real turn loop and public intents, with exact endpoints and negatives; no injected timing. |
| Peer/stack | 2 | Comparative near-miss peer / real stack case added in session 3 (see the peer/stack section) |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

Total: 8/10.

#### Intermittent timeout — cause and fix (session 3, lane C3)

Symptom: `counts a Tamer with [Tai Kamiya] inside a longer name but not a different Tamer`
timed out at 15 s intermittently, but only when `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` ran earlier in the
same worker.

Reproduction (3 runs):

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts \
  src/cards/BT17/BT17-019.test.ts src/cards/BT17/BT17-007.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
run 1 -> 2 failed | 129 passed (BT17-007 + BT17-019 timeouts, 31.1 s)
run 2 -> 131 passed (0.75 s)
run 3 -> 1 failed | 130 passed (0.6 s for BT17-007)
```

Cause: the peer/stack section above assumed `BT17-081` is never imported, so only its catalog
identity is in play. That holds when the file runs alone. `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` imports
every BT17 module, so in a shared worker `BT17-081`'s compiled effects are registered and raise
an optional prompt during `advance().runTurn(0)`. With no decision automation flags set nothing
answered it and the turn loop hung to the 15 s timeout.

Fix: pass `{ autoDeclineOptional: true, autoSelectCards: true }` to that fixture's
`setupEngine`. Declining does not touch the asserted clause — BT17-007's
`[Start of Your Main Phase]` return-from-trash is mandatory — so the endpoints (WarGreymon in
hand, trash empty) stand unchanged. No `it.skip`, no timeout bump. The peer claim itself is
unaffected: the substring gate still has to accept "Tai Kamiya & Matt Ishida" and reject
"Matt Ishida".

Result: 6 consecutive green runs of the trio, all ~0.6 s, no 15 s stall.

### BT17-008 — Guilmon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv3, Red, Virus, Reptile. Play cost 3, DP 1000. `evoCosts`: Red Lv2 for 0.
No printed `[Digivolve]` route, so `compiled.digivolutionRequirement` is absent.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When one of your [Calumon] or Tamers with [Takato Matsuki] in their name is played,` — a `whenPlayed` sub-trigger, own turn only, once per turn.
2. `delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 3000 DP or less.`
3. `If this effect didn't delete, gain 1 memory.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] While you have 0 or less memory, add 2000 to this Digimon's DP-based deletion effects' maximums.`

Catalog strings carry no U+00A0; the test asserts both texts byte-for-byte.

#### Defects found and fixed (IR changed)

1. `[Calumon]` is a bracketed name with no "in name", so it is literal card-name
   equality. The module used `match: "name"` (substring). Changed to
   `match: "nameExact"`. No near-name negative is possible from the catalog: the
   only cards whose `nameEn` contains "Calumon" are BT19-077 and EX2-045, both
   named exactly `Calumon`. `Tamers with [Takato Matsuki] in their name` is
   correctly left as substring `match: "name"`.
2. `condition: { kind: "memoryAtMost", value: 0 }` had no `controller`. Without
   it, `interpreter/conditions.ts` (`memoryAtMost`) reads the raw turn-relative
   `state.memory`, which is the OPPONENT's memory on the opponent's turn — wrong
   for an `[All Turns]` inherited effect that says "while YOU have 0 or less
   memory" (Q2712). Changed to `controller: "mine"`, which resolves the gauge
   against `ctx.source.ownerSeat`.

Both are compiled-IR changes, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` needs the
coordinator's set sync. `effects.json` was not touched.

Test defect fixed: the previous "raises a DP deletion threshold" test used
`BT1-010` (2000 DP) under the alias `fiveKTarget`. 2000 is already inside
Growlmon's printed 4000 maximum, so the test proved nothing about the +2000
bonus. It now uses `BT1-013` (5000 DP, inert), plus a positive-memory negative.

#### Q&A coverage

| Id | Covered by |
| --- | --- |
| Q2710 (mandatory when a legal target exists) | "deletes a 3000 DP Digimon when a Takato Matsuki Tamer is played, without gaining memory" — memory 10 → 7 (play cost only), no `+1`, target in trash. |
| Q2711 (a delete-immune target is still a legal choice and yields the memory) | "gains 1 memory when the only legal target cannot be deleted by opponent effects" — BT14-062 (`Restrict beDeleted`, `byOpponentEffectsOnly`) at `dp: 2000`; memory 10 → 7 → 8, Datamon survives. |
| Q2712 (owner-relative memory, 0 through 10 on the opponent's side) | IR fix `controller: "mine"` + "raises Growlmon's 4000 DP deletion maximum to 6000 while its owner has 0 memory" and its positive-memory negative. See gaps. |
| Q2713 (the bonus raises a printed numeric maximum) | Same pair: 5000 DP target deleted at memory 0, survives at memory 2. |
| Q2714 (no raise for a DP-relative threshold) | Engine-level, not card-level: `raiseDeletionDpCap` (`engine/effects/interpreter/targeting/permanents.ts:237-243`) returns the target unchanged for `relativeToSource`, and only ever raises `dp.op === "lte"` with a numeric `value`. BT17-008's own IR emits exactly that numeric form, asserted in the compiled-IR test. No BT17-008 fixture can exercise the relative branch because BT17-008 never produces one. |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 (trigger, source filter) | Takato play, Calumon play, non-Takato Tamer refusal | `SubTrigger` `whenPlayed`, `sourceFilter.or` of `nameExact` Calumon / `name` Takato Matsuki, `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| 1 (once per turn) | "fires once per turn and resets on the next own turn through the real turn loop" — three Takato plays across two of seat 0's turns via `startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| 2 (delete ≤ 3000) | 3000 DP boundary target deleted; 4000 DP target untouched | `Delete`, `dp: { op: "lte", value: 3000 }`, `controller: "opponent"`, `count: 1` |
| 3 (memory on no deletion) | no-legal-target case and immune-target case both land on memory `+1` | `GainMemory` with `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` |
| 4 (inherited +2000) | Growlmon digivolve at memory 0 deletes a 5000 DP Digimon; at memory 2 it survives and Growlmon takes its own +3000 DP fallback (8000) | `DeletionMaxDpModifier`, `amount: 2000`, `scope: "self"`, `duration: "permanent"` (a real `EffectDurationRef`), `memoryAtMost` with `controller: "mine"` |
| Evolution | red Lv2 egg (`BT1-001`) → BT17-008 in breeding for 0, stack identity asserted; blue Lv2 egg (`BT1-003`) refused | `evoCosts` Red Lv2 / 0 |

Fixtures use only inert main-deck Digimon (`BT1-009` 3000, `BT1-013` 5000,
`BT1-014` 4000) and list-form decks. No Digi-Egg in security or deck, no numeric
`security: <n>`, no injected timing (`fire`/`fireSubTrigger`/`fireTiming` are not
used anywhere in the file). Seat 0 always keeps a spare card in hand.

```
  -> clean
```

No `it.fails`/`it.skip` retained; neither known engine seam (`canPayCost` upTo,
`attackTargetMatchesFilter`) is reachable from this card.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q2712's opponent-turn half is argued from the interpreter source and the IR fix,
  not from a behavioural test: proving the sign flip needs a DP-based deletion that
  a BT17-008 carrier can fire during the opponent's turn, which BT17-008 and
  BT17-010 do not have.
- No substring-only positive exists for the Takato branch: the catalog has no Tamer
  whose name merely contains "Takato Matsuki". The branch is proven by a positive
  (BT17-080) and an unrelated-Tamer negative (BT1-085 Tai Kamiya).
- No near-name negative for `nameExact` Calumon exists in the catalog (see above).

None. `cards.json` matches the official text for BT17-008.

### BT17-009 — Flamemon

#### Card summary

Catalog: Digimon, Lv3, Red, play cost 3, DP 1000, form `Hybrid`, type `Wizard`,
evo costs Red Lv2 / Purple Lv2 for 0.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play]` Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck.
2. Add 1 card with the `[Hybrid]`/`[Ten Warriors]` trait **and** 1 Tamer card
   with an inherited effect among them to the hand.
3. Return the rest to the bottom of the deck.
4. Inherited `[On Deletion]` You **may** play 1 Tamer card with inherited
   effects from your hand without paying the cost.

No catalog discrepancy. Note that `[Hybrid]` lives in the catalog's `forms`
field rather than `types`; the engine's trait set is
`types ∪ forms ∪ attributes` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/context.ts:188`,
`detach.ts:73`), so `match: "trait"` correctly matches it — verified
behaviourally with `BT17-023` KendoGarurumon (`forms: ["Hybrid"]`).

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2715 | If only one of the two card types is revealed, you may still add that one. | `Q2715: adds the lone applicable card when only one of the three qualifies` — only a `[Hybrid]` card among the three; it is added and the other two are bottomed, with no decision left pending. |
| Q2716 | If both are revealed you **must** add as many as possible; you cannot add only one. | `Q2716: adds both applicable cards and bottoms only the remainder` — hand ends with exactly `BT17-023` and `BT17-083`, deck bottom holds only `BT1-009`. |
| Q2717 | "Tamer card with inherited effects" means "Inherited Effect" in the lower text; a `[Security]` effect in the lower text does not count. | `Q2717: a Tamer whose only lower text is a [Security] effect is not an eligible add` — `BT1-085` Tai Kamiya (`[Security]` lower text, empty `inheritedEffectText`) is bottomed, not added. Matches `interpreter/matching/definition.ts:242`, which gates `hasInheritedEffects` on `def.inheritedEffectText`. |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reveal top 3 | all three reveal tests (deck length and bottom order asserted) | `RevealAdd { revealCount: 3 }` |
| Add 1 `[Hybrid]`/`[Ten Warriors]` | `Q2715…`, `Q2716…`, `Q2717…`, `adds one Hybrid and one eligible Tamer from the top three` | `add[0].filter.nameOrTrait = [{ tokens: ["Hybrid","Ten Warriors"], match: "trait" }]`, `count: 1`, `to: "hand"` |
| Add 1 Tamer with an inherited effect | `Q2716…`, `Q2717…` | `add[1].filter = { kind: ["Tamer"], hasInheritedEffects: true }` |
| Both adds mandatory when available | `Q2716…` | two separate non-optional `add` entries |
| Return the rest to the bottom | `Q2715…` and `Q2717…` assert the exact resulting deck order | `rest: "deckBottom"` |
| Inherited `[On Deletion]` free Tamer play | `plays an inherited-effect Tamer when the host is naturally deleted in battle` (positive) and `inherited [On Deletion] stays silent when the hand holds no inherited-effect Tamer` (negative) | `{ trigger: "OnDeletion", isInherited: true, actions: [PlayWithoutCost { from: ["hand"], payCost: false, optional: true, target.filter = { zone: "hand", kind: ["Tamer"], hasInheritedEffects: true, controller: "mine" } }] }` |

Structural IR checks are retained as the first two tests. The module has exactly
one `registerIrCard("BT17-009", compiled)` and no `registerCard`;
`coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` are honest — every printed clause is
implemented and behaviourally proven.

##### Peer / comparative and stack evidence

- Comparative case inside one fixture (Q2717): the revealed set contains a
  `[Hybrid]` Digimon and a Tamer that looks eligible but is not, plus inert
  filler; only the genuinely eligible card moves to hand and the near-match is
  bottomed.
- Real evolution-stack case: the `[On Deletion]` clause is proven from inside a
  digivolution stack — `BT17-010` Growlmon on top of `BT17-001` and `BT17-009` —
  deleted by a real opponent attack through the public `attack` intent, not an
  injected timing. The negative uses the same stack with an ineligible Tamer in
  hand, so the two cases differ only in the hand card.
- Free play is proven free: memory is captured before the deletion and asserted
  unchanged afterwards, and the hand ends empty.

No Digi-Egg in security or deck (the only DigiEgg used, `BT17-001`, sits inside a

### BT17-011 — Agunimon

Lane 31. Digimon, Lv4, Red/Purple, DP 5000, play cost 6, form Hybrid.

#### Printed clauses

Effect text:

- `[Digivolve][Takuya Kanbara]: Cost 2` — named alternate route (base is a Tamer).
- `[Digivolve][BurningGreymon]: Cost 1` — named alternate route.
- `You may digivolve this card from your hand onto one of your red Tamers as if
  that card is a level 3 red Digimon.` — Static onto-red-Tamer route, `asLevel: 3`.
- `[When Digivolving] If [BurningGreymon] is in this Digimon's digivolution cards
  or you have a blue or green Digimon or Tamer, this Digimon may digivolve into
  [AncientGreymon] in the hand for a digivolution cost of 3, ignoring its
  digivolution requirements. If digivolved by this effect, delete this Digimon at
  the end of the turn.` — optional Digivolve (`costOverride: 3`,
  `ignoreRequirements`, `anyOf` condition) + conditional `DelayedDelete`.

Inherited: `[Your Turn] This Digimon gets +2000 DP.` (`ModifyDP` +2000, permanent).

#### Q&A coverage (12 ids)

- Q2725 (bonus draw on Tamer digivolve): covered — Takuya-route test asserts deck
  drops by 1 (bonus draw).
- Q2727 (Tamer becomes a digivolution card): covered — asserts the Takuya card
  stays as the single stack card under BT17-011.
- Q2728 (delete-at-end-of-turn baseline): covered — AncientGreymon path deletes
  the whole stack at turn end via `advance().runTurn(0)`.
- Q6555 (host gains inherited effect of Tamer under it) / inherited +2000 DP:
  covered by the inherited-DP test shape (base 4000 -> 6000 on turn, 4000 off).
- Q2723, Q2724 (Tamer treated as a Digimon; triggers fire; "can't digivolve"
  blocks it): partial — the onto-red-Tamer digivolve and the named-Takuya route
  are exercised behaviourally, but the "treated as a Digimon so when-digivolving
  triggers fire / a can't-digivolve lock blocks it" nuance is not asserted; no
  in-fixture lock card was added. Documented gap.
- Q2726 (summoning-sickness: cannot attack the turn it digivolves from a Tamer):
  not tested — attack-eligibility timing is engine-wide behaviour, not a printed
  clause of this card. Untestable here without asserting general turn rules.
- Q2729 (De-Digivolve to Tamer cancels the delete), Q2730 (BT3-109 interaction),
  Q2731 (delete skipped when digivolved at end-of-turn timing): not tested —
  each needs another card (a De-Digivolve source, BT3-109, BT18-088 end-of-turn
  attack) outside this lane's allowed files, and Q2731 depends on end-of-turn
  timing plumbing. Documented as out-of-scope for a single-card lane.
- Q4657 (cannot decline the 1st effect's digivolution once declared): not tested
  — the Static route is a normal declared digivolution; "cannot choose not to
  digivolve after reveal" is engine digivolution-procedure behaviour, not a
  branch of this IR.
- Q6554 (Digimon does NOT gain a Tamer's Security effect): not applicable to this
  card's own IR; no security clause on the Tamer sources used.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

- Named routes -> "gates both printed alternate routes on the exact name" ->
  `digivolutionRequirement` (now `namesExact`).
- Alias acceptance -> "accepts an official Takuya Kanbara alias on the exact
  route" -> `namesExact` + shared `effectiveNames.ts` alias for BT18-088.
- Takuya route behaviour -> "digivolves from Takuya Kanbara for cost 2, keeps the
  Tamer... and draws the bonus" -> `effects[0]` Static Digivolve + engine bonus draw.
- Generic red Tamer -> "uses the level-3 red catalog cost..." (cost 3) and
  "refuses a non-red Tamer" -> Static route `colors: ["Red"]`.
- AncientGreymon path -> "digivolves into AncientGreymon for 3... deletes it at
  end of turn" and "satisfies the condition through a blue Tamer" ->
  `effects[1]` Digivolve (`costOverride: 3`, `anyOf`) + `DelayedDelete`
  (`ifThisEffectDigivolved`).
- Condition false -> "does not offer the digivolve when neither branch holds".
- Optional decline -> "survives the turn when the optional digivolve is declined"
  (no delayed delete when not digivolved by the effect).
- Inherited DP -> "grants its inherited +2000 DP only on its controller's turn".

### BT17-012 — BurningGreymon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv4, Red/Purple, DP 6000, play cost 6, [Hybrid], type Dark Dragon.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve][Takuya Kanbara]: Cost 2` — alternate route from a Tamer base.
2. `[Digivolve][Agunimon]: Cost 1` — alternate route from a Lv4 Digimon base.
3. "You may digivolve this card from your hand onto one of your red Tamers as if
   that card is a level 3 red Digimon." — Tamer-onto generic path (level-3 cost).
4. `＜Raid＞` keyword.
5. `[When Attacking]` optional digivolve into a `[Hybrid]`-trait Digimon in hand,
   digivolution cost reduced by 1.
6. Inherited `[Your Turn] +2000 DP`.

#### Catalog / rules

Catalog matches the official text byte for byte, including the U+00A0 before
"trait" in the `[Hybrid] trait` clause (asserted in the test constant). No
discrepancy. Eleven Q&A ids surveyed via `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-012`.

#### Q&A coverage

- Q2734 (bonus draw on a Tamer digivolve) — behavioural, Takuya-route test.
- Q2735 (cannot attack the turn the Tamer base was placed) — behavioural.
- Q2736 (the Tamer becomes a digivolution card) — behavioural (stack assertion).
- Q2737 (attack digivolve must still meet digivolution requirements) —
  behavioural, EmperorGreymon (Hybrid but Lv5 base) refused.
- Q2738 (Raid vs When Attacking ordering) — Raid keyword publication asserted;
  the "turn player chooses order" arbitration is engine-level, not card IR, so
  only the keyword affordance is proven here.
- Q4658 (cannot decline the 1st effect once declared) — structural: the 1st
  effect compiles as a non-optional `Digivolve` action (no `optional` flag), and
  the `digivolve` intent has no decline branch; documented, not separately tested.
- Q6556 (Digimon does NOT gain a Tamer digivolution card's security effect) —
  behavioural: with BT17-079 underneath, nothing is played and the hand stays
  empty; the security clause lives in `effectText`, checked only in the security
  stack.
- Q6557 (Digimon DOES gain a Tamer digivolution card's inherited effect) —
  behavioural: +2000 DP granted only during the controller's turn.
- Q2731, Q2732, Q2733 — untestable on this card in isolation. They concern
  other cards' "treat the Tamer as a Digimon" effects and the "when a Digimon
  digivolves" triggers they raise (BT18-088 inherited attack, BT17-011's
  When-Digivolving into AncientGreymon). They need a second card's effect to
  drive them and belong to those cards' audits.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Takuya route cost 2 | "digivolves ... onto a [Takuya Kanbara] Tamer for 2" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` `namesExact:["Takuya Kanbara"], baseIsTamer` |
| Agunimon route cost 1 + near-name negative | "digivolves ... onto [Agunimon] for 1 but refuses ..." | `digivolutionRequirement[1]` `namesExact:["Agunimon"]` |
| Tamer-onto generic level-3 path | "digivolves onto an unnamed red Tamer for ... 3"; "refuses a Tamer that is not red" | `effects[0]` Static `Digivolve` `asLevel:3`, red-Tamer `onto` filter |
| Raid | "publishes Raid ..." | `effects[1]` keyword Raid |
| When Attacking -> Hybrid, cost -1 | "digivolves from an attack into a legal Hybrid for one less"; two negatives | `effects[2]` WhenAttacking `Digivolve` `reduceCost:1, optional`, Hybrid-trait `into` |
| Inherited +2000 DP | "grants its inherited +2000 DP only on its controller's turn" | `effects[3]` YourTurn inherited `ModifyDP 2000` |

### BT17-013 — WarGrowlmon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv5, Red, Virus, Cyborg, DP 7000, play cost 7, digivolve Red Lv4 cost 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[When Digivolving] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 6000 DP or less.`
2. `If this effect didn't delete, this Digimon gains ＜Security Attack +1＞ for the turn.`
3. Inherited: `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When an effect deletes an opponent's Digimon, you may unsuspend this Digimon with [Gallantmon] in its name.`

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2739 | With a legal target the deletion is mandatory; you cannot skip it to take the ＜Security Attack +1＞ branch. | `cannot decline the deletion while a legal target exists (Q2739)` — every optional prompt is declined (`autoDeclineOptional`) and the target still goes to trash with `keywordAmount(...,"SecurityAttack") === 0`. |
| Q2740 | A target that cannot be deleted is still a legal choice, and choosing it takes the "didn't delete" branch. | `grants Security Attack +1 when the chosen target cannot be deleted (Q2740)` — BT14-062 Datamon (6000 DP, `[All Turns] can't be deleted by your opponent's effects`) is preferred over a plain 3000 DP Digimon; both survive and Security Attack +1 lands. |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `deletes only an opposing Digimon at 6000 DP or less when digivolving` (in-range deleted, 6001 DP untouched); `cannot decline the deletion while a legal target exists (Q2739)` | `effects[0].actions[0]` `Delete`, `dp: { op: "lte", value: 6000 }`, `controller: "opponent"` |
| 2 | `gains Security Attack +1 when no opposing Digimon can be deleted`; `grants Security Attack +1 when the chosen target cannot be deleted (Q2740)` | `effects[0].actions[1]` `GainKeyword` `SecurityAttack +1`, `duration: "forTheTurn"`, `condition.kind: "ifThisEffectDidNotDelete"` |
| 3 | `unsuspends a Gallantmon host when another effect deletes an opponent Digimon`; `does not unsuspend a host whose name lacks Gallantmon`; `unsuspends only once per turn and resets on your next turn` | `effects[1]` `AllTurns`, `isInherited`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `SubTrigger onDeletionOf` with `deleteCause: "byEffect"`, `Unsuspend` `optional`, `condition.kind: "selfHasNameContaining" ["Gallantmon"]` |
| Printed text and costs | `matches the catalog printed text, stats and evolution cost` | catalog |

All behavioural cases drive `s.engine.applyIntent(0, { type: "digivolve", ... })` and settle before asserting. No injected timing (`fire`, `fireSubTrigger`, `fireTiming`) is used. `advance().verb.suspend` re-suspends the host between the two real deletions, and `advance().verb.deletePermanent(..., "byEffect")` supplies the third deletion after the turn loop — both are production primitives, used as plumbing only.

#### Once per turn

`unsuspends only once per turn and resets on your next turn` performs two real WarGrowlmon digivolutions in the same turn against two separate bases. The first deletion unsuspends the Gallantmon host; the host is re-suspended and the second effect deletion leaves it suspended (intermediate state asserted between the two). Two real turns then run through `advance().runTurn(0)` / `runTurn(1)`, after which a third effect deletion unsuspends it again.

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json          -> no error in BT17-013 files
```

- No retained red tests, no engine seam hit.
- Remaining gap: the inherited effect's "an effect deletes an opponent's Digimon" is only exercised with the controller's own effects; a deletion caused by the opponent's own effect is not covered.

`inheritedEffectText` carries a non-breaking space (U+00A0) between `[Gallantmon]` and `in its name`, where the official list uses a normal space. Cosmetic only; the catalog was not edited and the test asserts the stored bytes.

  your [Takuya Kanbara]s, digivolve it into this card as if that card is a level 4 red

| C2 | `[1]` `WhenDigivolving` -> `Delete` filter `dp lte 6000`, opponent Digimon | same test (3000 DP target deleted) + "leaves an opposing Digimon above 6000 DP alive" (7000 DP survives) |
| C3 | `[2]` `YourTurn` inherited `GrantStatic noSecurityOptionEffects`, `selfHasTrait` Hybrid/Ten Warriors, permanent | "suppresses only Option security effects, and only under a Hybrid or Ten Warriors host" |

#### Q&A coverage

  (BT12-088) is not suppressed while an Option (BT2-107) is.
- Q2742 (must digivolve for cost 3, not 4): covered. Memory 3 -> 0 and `costOverride: 3`.
- Q6558 (Tamer treated as Digimon; When Digivolving triggers fire): covered. The digivolve

- Q6559 (bonus draw even from a Tamer): covered. The single deck card reaches hand.
- Q6560 (a Digimon digivolved from a Tamer played this turn cannot attack this turn): NOT

  full turn loop that plays the Tamer this turn, digivolves, then declares an attack. Left as

- Q6561 (Tamer under a Digimon is a digivolution card): covered in part. Takuya is asserted

`it.fails("gains the digivolution-card Tamer's inherited effect after digivolving, per Q6563")`.

  its `[Your Turn] This Digimon gets +2000 DP` is conferred onto the host, so Aldamon reads
  10000 DP on its controller's turn.

- Not the harness: BT7-014's suite confirms an ordinary Digimon-source `[Your Turn]` +DP
  continuous applies in-harness, so the gap is specific to a Tamer digivolution card's
  inherited effect.

Digimon, Lv6, Red, Virus, Holy Warrior / Royal Knight, DP 11000, play cost 11, digivolve Red Lv5 cost 3. No inherited effect.

Printed clauses:

1. `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 8000 DP or less.`
2. `If this effect didn't delete, this Digimon gets +3000 DP and gains ＜Blocker＞ until the end of your opponent's turn.`
3. `[Your Turn] While you have 0 or less memory, this Digimon isn't affected by your opponent's effects.`

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2744 | With a legal target the deletion is mandatory; you cannot skip it to take the +3000 DP branch. | `cannot decline the deletion on attack while a legal target exists (Q2744)` — every optional prompt is declined, the 8000 DP target is trashed, DP stays 11000 and no ＜Blocker＞ is granted. |
| Q2745 | A deletion-proof target is still a legal choice, and choosing it takes the "didn't delete" branch. | `gains DP and Blocker when the chosen target cannot be deleted (Q2745)` — BT14-062 Datamon is preferred over a plain 8000 DP Digimon; both survive, DP becomes 14000 and ＜Blocker＞ is granted. |
| Q2746 | ST7-05's inherited memory gain resolves before ＜Retaliation＞, memory reaches 1, the `[Your Turn]` immunity is gone, and this Digimon is deleted. | `is deleted by Retaliation once an inherited memory gain lifts memory above 0 (Q2746)` — a real attack into BT5-080 Zanbamon (10000 DP, ＜Retaliation＞) at memory 0. With ST7-05 underneath, memory ends at 1 and Gallantmon is trashed; the comparative half without ST7-05 keeps memory at 0 and Gallantmon survives. |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 (When Digivolving) | `deletes an opposing 8000 DP Digimon through a natural evolution`; `digivolves from a Lv5 Red source ...` | `effects[0].actions[0]` `Delete`, `dp: { op: "lte", value: 8000 }` |
| 1 (When Attacking) | `cannot decline the deletion on attack while a legal target exists (Q2744)` | `effects[1].actions[0]` same shape under `trigger: "WhenAttacking"` |
| 2 | `gains DP and Blocker after a natural attack finds no opposing Digimon within range`; `gains DP and Blocker when the chosen target cannot be deleted (Q2745)`; `keeps the DP and Blocker grants through the opponent's turn` | `actions[1]` `ModifyDP +3000` and `actions[2]` `GainKeyword Blocker`, both `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"`, `condition.kind: "ifThisEffectDidNotDelete"`, on both triggers |
| 3 | `ignores an opponent security effect at 0 memory but not at 1 memory`; `is deleted by Retaliation ... (Q2746)` | `effects[2]` `YourTurn` `GrantImmunity` `immuneFrom: "opponentEffects"`, `condition: { kind: "memoryAtMost", value: 0, controller: "mine" }` |
| Printed text and costs | `matches the catalog printed text, stats and evolution cost` | catalog |

#### Evolution proof

`digivolves from a Lv5 Red source for its printed cost, with the source stack and bonus draw` uses the public `digivolve` intent from a BT17-013 base carrying BT1-015 underneath: memory drops by exactly 3 (3 → 0), `Permanent.stack` is exactly `["BT1-015", "BT17-013"]` (cards beneath the top card), and the digivolution draw puts the named deck card into hand at unchanged hand size. `refuses an illegal Lv3 digivolution source` proves the negative: the intent is refused, the base keeps its top card and memory is untouched.

#### Duration proof

`keeps the DP and Blocker grants through the opponent's turn` grants the pair on a real attack, runs the rest of the controller's turn and the whole opponent turn through `advance().runTurn`, and asserts the grants are still live during the opponent's turn and gone after it (DP back to 11000, no ＜Blocker＞). That confirms `untilOpponentTurnEnd` is a real `EffectDurationRef` and not a silently expiring string.

#### Immunity proof

`ignores an opponent security effect at 0 memory but not at 1 memory` builds the same board twice and drives a real attack whose security check flips ST22-08 Offensive Plug-In V (`[Security] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with the lowest DP`). At memory 0 Gallantmon survives; at memory 1 the identical flow deletes it. No injected timing is used anywhere in the file.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-016.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  Test Files 1 passed (1) | Tests 12 passed (12)
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-016.test.ts            -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-016.test.ts     -> correct format
git diff --check                                                     -> clean
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json          -> no error in BT17-016 files
```

#### Defects and gaps

- No defect found in the module. `BT17-016.ts` was not edited, so the compiled IR is unchanged and `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is unaffected.
- No retained red tests, no engine seam hit. The Q2746 ordering (turn-player trigger first, then ＜Retaliation＞) resolves correctly in the engine today.
- Remaining gap: ＜Blocker＞ is asserted as a granted keyword and through its duration, but no test forces an actual block with it; the "isn't affected" immunity is proved against a deletion effect only, not against a DP-reduction or restriction effect.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `effectText` matches the official wording, `inheritedEffectText` is absent as printed.

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog/rules | 2 | Printed text, stats and evo cost asserted; all three assigned Q&A ids covered by behavioural tests. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a named IR node on both triggers, with duration and condition kinds checked. |
| Behavioural proof | 2 | Public intents only, exact endpoints (trash membership, board occupancy, DP, keyword, memory, no pending decision), plus a real evolution route with cost, stack and draw, and an illegal-source negative. |
| Peer/stack proof | 2 | Deletion-proof vs plain target in one fixture; memory 0 vs memory 1 on identical boards; ST7-05 vs BT17-013 in the digivolution stack for Q2746. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### BT17-017 — AncientGreymon

#### Card

Digimon, Lv6, Red/Purple, play cost 12, DP 12000, Mega, Vaccine,
types Ancient Dragon / Ten Warriors. Evo costs: Red Lv5 for 4, Purple Lv5 for 4.

1. `＜Security Attack +1＞`
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with as much or less DP than this Digimon.`
3. `[On Deletion] Return 1 Tamer card and 1 [Hybrid] trait Digimon card from your trash to the hand. Then, you may play 1 Tamer card from your hand without paying the cost.`
4. `[DigiXros -3] [Agunimon] x [BurningGreymon]`

No inherited effect. No Q&A ids listed for this card, and `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-017`
returns no rulings, so there is nothing to cover.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 Security Attack +1 | `models Security Attack +1 ...` (structure); `checks two security cards on a natural attack from Security Attack +1` (behaviour: 3 security -> 1 left, 2 cards in opponent trash) | `effects[0]` Static, `keywords[{SecurityAttack, amount 1}]` |
| 2 On Play delete | `deletes an opposing Digimon at or below its DP through natural play`; `leaves an opposing Digimon with more DP alone and takes only the eligible one` (comparative: 13000 survives, 10000 deleted) | `effects[1]` OnPlay -> Delete, `dp {op lte, relativeToSource}` |
| 2 When Digivolving delete | `digivolves naturally from a red level-5 and applies the DP-relative deletion`; negative `refuses a level 4 source that no printed evolution cost accepts` | `effects[2]` WhenDigivolving -> Delete |
| 3 On Deletion returns + optional Tamer play | `returns the required Tamer and Hybrid cards after a natural battle deletion` (accepted branch: Tamer played to the board); `still returns both trash cards when the optional Tamer play is declined` (declined branch) | `effects[3]` OnDeletion -> Return x2 (`to: "hand"`), PlayWithoutCost `optional: true, payCost: false` |
| 4 DigiXros -3 | `keeps the DigiXros recipe ...` (structure); `DigiXroses Agunimon and BurningGreymon for the printed -3 reduction` (real DigiXros intent, stack identity, memory 6 -> 0) | `digiXrosRequirement` count 3, materials `[{names:["Agunimon"]},{names:["BurningGreymon"]}]` |

`[Agunimon]` / `[BurningGreymon]` are exact bracketed names; the DigiXros material

### BT17-018 — Gallantmon: Crimson Mode

#### Card

Digimon, Lv7, Red, play cost 8, DP 15000, Mega, Virus, type Holy Warrior.
Catalog evo cost: Red Lv6 for 5. `isAce: true`, `overflowMemory: 5`.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.6 w/[Gallantmon] in its name: Cost 4`
2. `[Hand] [Counter] ＜Blast Digivolve＞`
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Choose any number of your opponent's Digimon whose total DP adds up to 15000 or less and delete them.`
4. `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] For every 10 cards in both player's trash, trash 1 card from the top of your opponent's security stack.`

No inherited effect.

#### Q&A

- **Q2747** (2024-03-28): "for every 10 cards in both players' trashes" is the
  SUM of both trashes, not each separately. Covered by
  `trashes two security cards from a natural attack after counting both players' trashes (Q2747)`
  (10 cards in each trash -> 20 total -> 2 security cards trashed) and re-exercised
  by the once-per-turn test, which also runs at 20 total. The IR encodes this as
  `scaling: { per: 10, unit: "cards", filter: { zone: "trash", controller: "any" } }` —
  `controller: "any"` is the summing form.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 Digivolve Lv.6 w/[Gallantmon]: Cost 4 | `digivolves through the printed Lv.6 [Gallantmon] route for cost 4 with the bonus draw` (ST7-09 Gallantmon base, memory 4 -> 0, stack = base instance, 1 card drawn, deck 12 -> 11) | `digivolutionRequirement [{ level: 6, names: ["Gallantmon"], cost: 4, isAlternate: true }]` — `names` is the SUBSTRING matcher in `DigivolutionRequirement`, which is correct for "in its name" |
| 1 negatives / comparative | `still digivolves from a Lv.6 red Digimon without [Gallantmon] in its name at the catalog cost 5` (BT8-017, memory 5 -> 0); `does not apply the reduced cost to a Lv.6 source without [Gallantmon] in its name` (`useAlternateCost` hint ignored, 5 charged); `refuses an off-color Lv.6 source that no printed route accepts` (BT1-080 green, refused, memory untouched) | as above + catalog `evoCosts` |
| 2 [Hand] [Counter] Blast Digivolve | `Blast Digivolves from hand during a natural Counter Timing and deletes up to 15000 DP` (real `counterWindowOpened`, `respondCounter`, memory stays 0) | `effects[0]` Counter, `isFromHand: true`, `keywords [{BlastDigivolve}]` |
| 3 On Play / When Digivolving total-DP delete | Blast test above (9000 + 6000 = 15000 deleted, 1000 and 3000 survive); `deletes only one Digimon when a second would push the total past 15000` (two 9000s -> exactly one deleted) | `effects[1]` OnPlay and `effects[2]` WhenDigivolving -> Delete, `count: "all"`, `totalDpCap: 15000` |
| 4 When Attacking, Once Per Turn, scaled security trash | `trashes two security cards ... (Q2747)`; `trashes security only on the first attack each turn and again on the next own turn` (8 security: attack 1 -> 5 left = 2 effect + 1 check; attack 2 same turn -> 4 left = check only; after a real turn loop back to seat 0, attack 3 -> 1 left = 2 effect + 1 check) | `effects[3]` WhenAttacking, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `SecurityManipulation { op: "trashTop", controller: "opponent", amount: 1, scaling }` |

```
  -> 1 file passed, 11 tests passed

pnpm exec oxlint <files> -> exit 0; pnpm exec oxfmt <files> -> clean; git diff --check -> clean
```

#### Changes

Test file only. Added the printed alternate-route digivolve (with cost, stack
identity and bonus draw), a same-level comparative route at the catalog cost 5,
two negatives, the `totalDpCap` boundary case, and the once-per-turn refusal plus
next-own-turn reset through `advance(...).runTurn`. The module (`BT17-018.ts`) was
not touched, so the compiled IR is unchanged and `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is
unaffected.

#### Defects and gaps

- No defects found; no test retained red.
- Observed engine behaviour worth recording: `useAlternateCost: true` is a hint
  the server revalidates. When no printed alternate path accepts the source, the
  engine silently falls back to the catalog route and charges the catalog cost
  rather than refusing the intent. The test asserts the charged cost (5, not 4)
  instead of a refusal, so the reduced route cannot be claimed by an ineligible
  source. The refusal negative uses an off-color Lv.6 base, where both routes are
  illegal.
- The once-per-turn reset uses `advance(...).verb.unsuspend` between real attacks
  (allowed plumbing) and real `runTurn` calls for the turn rotation; no injected
  timing is used anywhere in the file.
- Gap: `isAce` / `overflowMemory: 5` is catalog-driven and is not represented in
  the compiled IR, and no test in this lane exercises the ACE overflow. It is not
  a printed `effectText` clause, so it is out of this card's clause list, but a
  coordinator-level ACE check would be worth having.
- Fixtures use inert main-deck Digimon (BT1-009) and inert Lv6 bodies (BT8-017,
  BT1-080); no Digi-Egg in security or deck and no numeric `security: <n>` form.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. The catalog `effectText` matches the official text for all four clauses.

### BT17-019 — Gabumon

#### Card

- Digimon, Lv3, Blue, play cost 3, DP 1000, [Reptile], Rookie/Data.
- evoCosts: Blue Lv2 cost 0, Red Lv2 cost 0.
- Printed clauses:
  1. `[Digivolve][Tsunomon]: Cost 0` — alternate digivolution route from any Tsunomon.
  2. `[Start of Your Main Phase] If you have a Tamer with [Matt Ishida] in its name, ＜Draw 1＞.`
  3. Inherited: `[End of Your Turn] This Digimon and another of your Digimon may DNA digivolve into a Digimon card in the hand.`

Note: the catalog text uses a non-breaking space after `[Matt Ishida]`; the test asserts the exact bytes.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2748 | Inherited DNA digivolve happens at end of *your* turn, using the carrier plus one other own Digimon, into a hand Digimon with [DNA Digivolution] | "naturally DNA digivolves at end of turn using itself and another Digimon" |
| Q2749 | Cannot DNA digivolve into a hand Digimon without [DNA Digivolution] | "does not DNA digivolve into a hand Digimon without a DNA digivolution requirement" |
| Q2750 | Cannot use materials outside the [DNA Digivolution] specification | same test (BT1-069 Ogremon + BT22-022 host cannot reach BT1-070 Kuwagamon, which has no DNA requirement) |

Q2749 and Q2750 share one negative: both reduce to "the printed [DNA Digivolution] requirement is not ignorable". The engine refuses the whole action rather than reporting which half failed, so a finer split is not observable from the public API.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Digivolve][Tsunomon]: Cost 0 | "accepts the [Tsunomon] Cost 0 route from an off-color Tsunomon in breeding" (ST16-01, Purple — illegal on the printed Blue/Red Lv2 routes) plus "rejects an off-color non-Tsunomon egg as a digivolution source" (BT17-006 Bowmon, Purple Lv2) | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ names: ["Tsunomon"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` |
| [Start of Your Main Phase] conditional draw | "draws at the start of the main phase when Matt is present" (BT1-086), "treats [Matt Ishida] as a substring of the Tamer name" (BT17-081 Tai Kamiya & Matt Ishida), "does not draw when no Matt Ishida Tamer is present" (BT1-085 Tai Kamiya) | `effects[0]` StartOfYourMainPhase / Draw 1 / `youHave` Tamer with `match: "name"` |
| Inherited end-of-turn DNA digivolve | positive and negative DNA tests above | `effects[1]` EndOfYourTurn, `isInherited: true`, DnaDigivolve with `isSelfRef` + `excludeSelf` materials into a hand Digimon with `hasDnaDigivolutionRequirement` |

`match: "name"` (substring) is correct here — the printed wording is "with [Matt Ishida] **in its name**", which the brief classifies as substring, not `nameExact`. The set-wide `nameExact` defect does not apply to this module.

#### Commands

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-019.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
```
Result: 10 passed (10). oxlint clean, oxfmt clean, `git diff --check` clean.

#### Defects and gaps

- No defect found; the module IR was not changed, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is unaffected.
- Cost proof for the Tsunomon route is structural in one respect: a Lv2 source only exists in breeding, where digivolution costs 0 memory regardless of route, so route legality (off-color Tsunomon accepted, off-color non-Tsunomon refused) is what the tests prove, not the 0 memory figure.
- No bonus-draw check: breeding digivolution does not grant the draw.
- The 15 s timeout on `treats [Matt Ishida] as a substring of the Tamer name` in
  `logs/restart-checkpoint-collection.log` is **not a BT17-019 defect**. Lane S isolated it to
  `BT17-085.test.ts:179` (`does not treat an unrelated Digimon as Sakuyamon ...`), which asserts
  synchronously and leaves an unanswered `optional` decision resolving in the background; that
  starves the microtask polling in `advance.waitForMainPhase` for whichever file runs next in
  the same worker. BT17-019 passes alone (10 passed, ~0.4 s) and in the full `src/cards/BT17`
  gate. No change made to `BT17-019.ts` or `BT17-019.test.ts`. Details and the per-test
  isolation table: `SHARED-NAME-ALIAS-MECHANISM.md` section 6.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog/rules | 2 | Full catalog match asserted byte-exact; all three Q&A read and mapped |
| IR trace | 2 | Every printed clause mapped to a named IR node; substring-vs-exact classification verified |
| Behavioural proof | 2 | Real turn loop, public intents, endpoint assertions on hand, deck, memory, stack; legal and illegal digivolution sources |
| Peer/stack proof | 2 | Real evolution-stack cases (breeding stack identity preserved; DNA stack contains both materials) plus a comparative Tamer pair (Matt / Tai & Matt / Tai) |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane |

Total 8/10.

#### Intermittent timeout — cause and fix (session 3, lane C3)

Symptom: `treats [Matt Ishida] as a substring of the Tamer name` timed out at 15 s in about
one run in two, but only when `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` ran earlier in the same worker.

Reproduction (3 runs):

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts \
  src/cards/BT17/BT17-019.test.ts src/cards/BT17/BT17-007.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
run 1 -> 2 failed | 129 passed (BT17-019 + BT17-007 timeouts, 31.1 s)
run 2 -> 131 passed (0.75 s)
run 3 -> 1 failed | 130 passed (BT17-019 timeout, 15.7 s)
```

Cause: the fixture puts `BT17-081` "Tai Kamiya & Matt Ishida" on the board purely as a
catalog-identity peer. The catalog-sync file imports every BT17 module, so in a shared worker
`BT17-081`'s own compiled effects are registered too and raise an optional "use this effect?"
prompt during `advance().runTurn(0)`. The test set no decision automation flags, so nothing
answered the prompt and the turn loop waited out the 15 s timeout. It is registration order,
not timing: alone the module is never registered and no prompt exists.

Fix: pass `{ autoDeclineOptional: true, autoSelectCards: true }` to `setupEngine`. Declining
leaves the asserted flow untouched — BT17-019's `[Start of Your Main Phase] ＜Draw 1＞` is
mandatory — so the endpoint assertions (hand +1, deck empty) are unchanged and unweakened.
No `it.skip`, no timeout bump.

Result: 6 consecutive green runs of the trio, all ~0.6 s, no 15 s stall.

### BT17-020 — Strabimon

#### Card

- Digimon, Lv3, Blue, play cost 3, DP 1000, [Beastkin], Hybrid/Variable.
- evoCosts: Blue Lv2 cost 0, Yellow Lv2 cost 0.
- Printed clauses:
  1. `[On Play] Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck. Add 1 card with the [Hybrid]/[Ten Warriors] trait and 1 Tamer card with an inherited effect among them to the hand. Return the rest to the bottom of the deck.`
  2. Inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] You may play 1 Tamer card with an inherited effect from your hand with the play cost reduced by 2.`

The catalog text uses a non-breaking space after `[Ten Warriors]`; the test asserts the exact bytes.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2751 | If only one of the two card types is revealed, you may still add that one | "adds the single applicable card when only one is revealed (Q2751)" |
| Q2752 | If both types are revealed you must add as many as possible — you cannot add only one | "adds a Hybrid and an eligible Tamer from the top three" and "returns the unpicked revealed cards to the bottom of the deck (Q2752)" |
| Q2753 | "Tamer card with inherited effects" means "Inherited Effect" in the lower text; a Tamer whose lower text is only a [Security] effect does not qualify | "does not treat a Tamer whose lower text is only [Security] as an inherited-effect target" (BT20-089) and the existing BT1-085 case (no lower text at all) |

Q2752's "must add as many as possible" is verified positively (both eligible cards land in hand and only the third goes to the deck bottom). The engine's automated selection has no public intent for declining a mandatory add, so the "you cannot add only one" refusal is not directly observable; the positive form is the strongest available evidence.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reveal top 3 | reveal tests above; deck length drops to `size - 3` before the returns | `effects[0]` OnPlay `RevealAdd` with `revealCount: 3` |
| Add 1 [Hybrid]/[Ten Warriors] | BT17-023 KendoGarurumon (Hybrid form) added | `add[0]` `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Hybrid","Ten Warriors"], match: "trait" }]` — exact-trait matching, correct for "with the [X] trait" |
| Add 1 Tamer with an inherited effect | BT17-083 Koji added; BT20-089 and BT1-085 refused | `add[1]` `kind: ["Tamer"], hasInheritedEffects: true` |
| Return the rest to the bottom | "returns the unpicked revealed cards to the bottom of the deck (Q2752)" asserts the exact resulting deck order | `rest: "deckBottom"` |
| Inherited [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] play a Tamer for 2 less | "plays an inherited-effect Tamer for 2 less when its inherited host attacks" (memory 2 -> 0 for a cost-4 Tamer) and "fires the inherited play only once per turn and resets on the next own turn" | `effects[1]` WhenAttacking, `isInherited: true`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `PlayWithoutCost` from hand with `reduceCostBy: 2`, `optional: true` |

`match: "trait"` is exact, which is what "with the [Hybrid]/[Ten Warriors] trait" prints. The set-wide `match: "name"` substring defect does not apply to this module — it carries no name reference.

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-020.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
```
Result: 10 passed (10). oxlint clean, oxfmt clean, `git diff --check` clean.

#### Defects and gaps

- No defect found; the module IR was not changed, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is unaffected.
- The once-per-turn reset test seeds `s.state.memory = 8` after each main phase begins so the cost-2 Tamer play does not hand the turn over mid-test; the turn boundary itself is driven by the real turn loop (`startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase`), and the second attack in the same turn uses `advance(...).verb.unsuspend` as plumbing only.
- Not covered: the [Hybrid] slot proved via a Hybrid-form card only; no [Ten Warriors]-trait-only card was exercised.

None.
