### BT17-043 — Terriermon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv3, Green, Vaccine, Beast. Play cost 3, DP 1000, evolve Green Lv2 for 0.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When an effect plays one of your [Terriermon]/[Lopmon]
   or green Tamers, you may suspend 1 of your opponent's Digimon.`
   - C1a trigger source: an own Digimon named exactly Terriermon or Lopmon, played by an effect.
   - C1b trigger source: an own green Tamer, played by an effect.
   - C1c effect: optional suspend of 1 opponent Digimon.
   - C1d frequency: once per turn, on your turn only.
2. Inherited: `[All Turns] While this Digimon is suspended, it gets +1000 DP.`

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2797 | The `[Your Turn]` effect does trigger when this card is played by another card's effect. | "suspends 1 opponent Digimon when another card's effect plays a [Terriermon], per Q2797" — BT17-049's `[When Digivolving]` plays BT17-043 out of the trash and the opponent's Digimon suspends. |

No BT17-043 banlist entry (BT17-069 is the set's only one).

#### Defect found and fixed

The printed reference `[Terriermon]/[Lopmon]` is an exact-name reference, but the IR
compiled it as `match: "name"` (substring). That is behaviourally wrong, not merely
cosmetic: the catalog contains `Terriermon (X Antibody)` (BT16-038) and
`Terriermon Assistant` (BT5-046, EX4-033), all of which a substring match accepts.

Fixed in `apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-043.ts` to `match: "nameExact"`.

Proof the change is behavioural, not cosmetic: with `match: "name"` restored, the
new test "does not trigger on a played [Terriermon Assistant]" fails (the opponent's
Digimon suspends); with `nameExact` it passes. Verified by a temporary local revert.

`EX4-033 Terriermon Assistant` self-treats its name as `[Terriermon]`, so the negative
test deliberately uses `BT5-046`, which does not.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity, printed text, whole IR | "matches the catalog identity, printed text and the complete IR contract" (`toEqual` on `compiled.effects`) | both effects |
| C1a + Q2797 | "suspends 1 opponent Digimon when another card's effect plays a [Terriermon], per Q2797" | `SubTrigger whenPlayed`, `sourceFilter.or[0]` |
| C1a exactness (negative / peer) | "does not trigger on a played [Terriermon Assistant]: the printed name reference is exact" | `nameOrTrait[].match: "nameExact"` |
| C1b + C1d + reset | "also triggers on an effect-played green Tamer, but only once per turn, and resets next turn" | `sourceFilter.or[1]`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| C1a origin (negative) | "does not trigger when a [Terriermon] is played from hand instead of by an effect" | `sourceFilter.byEffect: true` |
| C1d turn gate (negative) | "stays silent on the opponent's turn even when an own effect plays a [Terriermon]" | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| C1c | Same tests: exactly 1 of 2 opponent Digimon suspended, own Terriermon untouched | `Suspend` `controller: "opponent"`, `count: 1` |
| C1c declined branch | "leaves the opponent's board untouched when the optional suspend is declined" | `Suspend.optional: true` |
| 2 | "grants the inherited +1000 DP only while the host is suspended" | `Aura` / `selfIsSuspended` |

Endpoints asserted: instance ids by zone (trash -> battle area for the effect-played
card, hand contents after the intent), memory after each intent, opponent suspend
count over two distinct opponent Digimon, `pendingDecision === undefined`, and DP on
both an aura-carrying host and a peer control permanent.

The once-per-turn test drives real turn flow: ST17-02's `[Main]` effect plays a green
Tamer (suspend 1), BT17-049 then effect-plays a Terriermon in the same turn (no second
suspend), then `advance(...).runTurn(0)` / `runTurn(1)` / `runOneTurn()` +
`waitForMainPhase(0)` reaches the next own turn, where a fresh effect-play suspends
again. No injected timing is used; `advance(...).verb.unsuspend` only clears the
opponent's board between turns so the next suspend is observable.

#### Peer / stack proof

- Comparative case in the same fixture: `BT5-046 Terriermon Assistant` versus
  `BT17-043 Terriermon` through the identical BT17-049 play route.
- Stack case: the inherited test seeds `BT17-046` with `BT17-043` underneath, with a
  second `BT17-046` on the board carrying nothing as the control for the aura.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-043.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 8 passed (8)
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json
  -> no error in BT17-043.ts / BT17-043.test.ts
     (pre-existing, other lane: src/cards/BT17/BT17-058.test.ts(271,89) TS2551 `suspended`)
pnpm exec oxfmt <4 files>            -> clean
pnpm exec oxlint <4 files>           -> no findings
git diff --check -- <4 files>        -> clean
```

#### Follow-up round: gaps closed

All three gaps named in the first round are now covered.

- `[Your Turn]` negative: seat 1 takes a real turn and attacks seat 0's suspended
  `BT17-046 Gargomon`. Its `[On Deletion]` plays a `[Terriermon]` out of seat 0's trash —
  seat 0's own effect-play, exactly the qualifying event, but on the opponent's turn.
  The Terriermon reaches the battle area and seat 1's bystander stays unsuspended (the
  only suspended seat-1 permanent is the attacker, suspended by attacking).
- `byEffect` origin: a `[Terriermon]` played from hand with the ordinary `playCard`
  intent produces no suspend and no decision, while the identical board played by
  BT17-049's effect does.
- Declined branch: the play prompt is accepted and only the suspend prompt is declined,
  by answering `optional` decisions by hand (`declineOnlyTheSuspend`). `autoDeclineOptional`
  cannot express this — it would also decline the play the suspend depends on. The
  Terriermon is on the board, no opponent Digimon is suspended, nothing is left pending.

#### Remaining gaps

None material. Every printed clause, both trigger branches, both gates on the trigger
(`byEffect`, `[Your Turn]`), the frequency limit with a real reset, and both branches of
the optional suspend are proven behaviourally through public intents and real turn flow.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `effectText` and `inheritedEffectText` match the official printed text byte for
byte (asserted in the identity test).

#### IR changed

Yes: `match: "name"` -> `match: "nameExact"`. `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red
until the coordinator runs the set sync. `effects.json` was not touched.

| Peer / stack | 2 | Terriermon Assistant comparative in the same fixture, plus a real evolution-stack case with a control peer. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### BT17-044 — Morphomon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv3, Green, Vaccine, Insectoid. Play cost 3, DP 1000, evolve Green Lv2 for 0.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Your Turn] When this Digimon would digivolve into [Eosmon], reduce the digivolution
   cost by 1.`
   - C1a target: a digivolution whose destination is named exactly Eosmon.
   - C1b: reduce the cost by 1.
2. Inherited: `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When another [Eosmon] is played, this Digimon
   may digivolve into [Eosmon] in your hand with the digivolution cost Reduced by 3.`
   - C2a trigger: another (not this) Eosmon is played, on your turn.
   - C2b: optional digivolve of the host into an Eosmon in hand.
   - C2c: cost reduced by 3.
   - C2d: once per turn.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q2798 | The inherited effect does not let the host ignore digivolution requirements. | "still obeys the digivolution requirements of the hand [Eosmon], per Q2798" — a Lv4 host with `BT17-076` (Lv6, requires a Lv5 `[Eosmon]`) in hand does not digivolve when a second Eosmon is played; the card stays in hand and the host keeps its top card and stack. |

No BT17-044 banlist entry (BT17-069 is the set's only one).

#### Clause-1 reduction: verified working, not a defect (follow-up round 3)

Lane 20 (BT17-074) reported that BT17-044's clause 1 -- "reduce the digivolution cost by
1 when digivolving into [Eosmon]" -- appeared not to apply: memory dropped the full 2 in
its BT17-074 play tests.

Investigated with a comparative test that runs the same Morphomon -> BT17-074 digivolve
twice: once from `BT6-047` (a plain Morphomon whose only effect is `[On Deletion]`, the
control) and once from `BT17-044`. The control pays the full 2; BT17-044 pays 1. The
reduction fires exactly as printed.

Verdict: **not a BT17-044 IR defect and not an engine seam.** Clause 1 is `isSelfRef` --
it reduces only when BT17-044 is the Digimon that digivolves (its own top-card, battle-area
main effect). Lane 20's setup did not have BT17-044 as the active digivolving Digimon
(BT17-044 buried as a digivolution card, a plain-Morphomon source, or a digivolve from
breeding where main effects are inactive), so the self-ref reduction correctly did not
apply there. BT17-044.ts is unchanged.

#### Defect found and fixed

All three printed `[Eosmon]` references compiled as `match: "name"` (substring). The
printed bracket form is an exact-name reference. Fixed to `match: "nameExact"` in
`apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-044.ts` (the `into` filter of the cost replacement, the
inherited `sourceFilter`, and the inherited `Digivolve.into` filter).

Honest note on impact: today the catalog holds no card whose `nameEn` contains "Eosmon"
as a proper substring (the seven Eosmon are all named exactly "Eosmon"), so this fix is
correctness-preserving rather than behaviour-changing at the current card pool. It is
still the right compilation and it guards against future prints.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity, printed text, whole IR | "matches the catalog identity, printed text and the complete IR contract" (`toEqual`) | both effects |
| C1a + C1b | "reduces its own [Eosmon] digivolution from 2 memory to 1" | `Replacement wouldDigivolve` / `mode: "reduceCost"`, `amount: 1` |
| C1a negative (peer) | "does not reduce a digivolution into a Digimon that is not named [Eosmon]" (BT17-046 Gargomon costs the full 2) | `into.nameOrTrait` |
| C1a + C1b comparative control | "reduces the [Eosmon] digivolution by exactly 1 versus a plain Morphomon control" (BT6-047 -> BT17-074 costs 2; BT17-044 -> BT17-074 costs 1) | `Replacement wouldDigivolve` / `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` |
| C2a + C2b + C2c | "digivolves the host into a hand [Eosmon] for 3 less when another [Eosmon] is played" | inherited `SubTrigger whenPlayed` -> `Digivolve` `from: ["hand"]`, `reduceCost: 3` |
| C2b legality (Q2798) | "still obeys the digivolution requirements of the hand [Eosmon], per Q2798" | `Digivolve` route validation |
| C2a controller + turn gate | "does not fire when the opponent plays their own [Eosmon] on their own turn" | `sourceFilter.controllerDefault: "mine"`, `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| C2b declined branch | "leaves memory and the stack untouched when the inherited digivolve is declined" | `Digivolve.optional: true` |
| C2d | "fires the inherited digivolve once per turn and resets on the next own turn" | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

Endpoints asserted: memory after each intent (the discriminator for both reductions —
5 -> 4 with the reduction vs 5 -> 3 without; 6 -> 2 for a play-4 plus a 3-3 digivolve),
`Permanent.stack` card ids in order (digivolution cards only, top card excluded),
`topCard` instance ids, hand membership by instance id, the digivolution bonus draw
(hand length 2 and deck emptied), and `pendingDecision === undefined`.

The once-per-turn test is a real turn: play Eosmon #1 (host digivolves to BT17-075),
play Eosmon #2 the same turn with a now-legal `BT17-076` in hand (nothing happens —
the effect is spent), then `runTurn(0)` / `runTurn(1)` / `runOneTurn()` +
`waitForMainPhase(0)`, and on the next own turn a third Eosmon play digivolves the host
into `BT17-076`. The second play is the strongest form of the refusal: the route it
would have taken is legal, so only the frequency gate can be stopping it. No injected
timing anywhere in the file.

#### Peer / stack proof

- Comparative case: `BT17-046 Gargomon` (Green Lv4, evolve Green Lv3 for 2) against
  `BT17-074 Eosmon` (evolve `[Morphomon]` for 2) from the identical board — same source
  cost, only the destination name differs.
- Real evolution stack: every inherited test seeds `BT17-074` with `BT17-044` beneath
  and asserts the stack grows to `["BT17-044", "BT17-074", "BT17-075"]` across turns.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-044.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 9 passed (9)
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json
  -> no error in BT17-044.ts / BT17-044.test.ts
     (pre-existing, other lane: src/cards/BT17/BT17-058.test.ts(271,89) TS2551 `suspended`)
pnpm exec oxfmt <4 files>            -> clean
pnpm exec oxlint <4 files>           -> no findings
git diff --check -- <4 files>        -> clean
```

#### Follow-up round: gaps closed

- `[Your Turn]` and the controller gate: seat 1 takes a real turn and plays its own
  `BT17-074 Eosmon`. Seat 0's Morphomon-carrying host does not digivolve, the
  `BT17-075` stays in hand, and nothing is left pending. This is the only constructible
  opponent-turn Eosmon play; the clause-1 cost reduction has no opponent-turn analogue
  because a player cannot digivolve on the opponent's turn without a Counter/Blast
  vehicle, none of which routes into `[Eosmon]`.
- Declined branch: with `autoDeclineOptional`, playing a second Eosmon leaves the host
  at `BT17-074`, the stack at `["BT17-044"]`, the `BT17-075` in hand, and memory down
  only by the 4 the play itself cost.

#### Remaining gaps

- `excludeSelf: true` is not observable at this card pool. The inherited effect only
  exists while BT17-044 is a digivolution card, so its host permanent can never be the
  "played [Eosmon]" that would exercise the exclusion. It rests on the IR assertion.
- `nameExact` likewise cannot be proven behaviourally: no catalog card has "Eosmon" as
  a proper substring of its name, so substring and exact matching are indistinguishable
  today. It rests on the IR assertion.

Both are limits of the card pool, not untested behaviour that a fixture could reach.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `effectText` and `inheritedEffectText` match the printed text byte for byte,
including the capitalised "Reduced by 3" in the inherited line, which is how the card
is printed and how the catalog stores it.

#### IR changed

Yes: three `match: "name"` -> `match: "nameExact"`. `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be
red until the coordinator runs the set sync. `effects.json` was not touched.

| --- | --- | --- |

| IR trace | 2 | Whole `compiled.effects` asserted with `toEqual`; single `registerIrCard`, `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`. |
| Behavioural proof | 2 | Both clauses, both reductions with memory discriminators, the Q2798 legality limit, the controller and `[Your Turn]` gates proven negatively across a real opponent turn, both branches of the optional digivolve, and once-per-turn with a real next-own-turn reset. The two residual items are unobservable at this card pool, not untested. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Gargomon comparative in the same board shape plus a three-deep real evolution stack. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### BT17-045 — Argomon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv.4, Green/Purple, play cost 5, DP 5000, Mutant/Virus/Champion.
EvoCosts: Green Lv.3 cost 3, Purple Lv.3 cost 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.3 [Argomon]: Cost 2` — alternate digivolution route.
2. `[When Digivolving] If you don't have [Rhythm], you may play 1 [Rhythm] from
   your hand without paying the cost.` — optional, gated on a condition.
3. Inherited: `[On Deletion] Gain 1 memory.`

#### Q&A ids covered

None listed for this card. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-045` returns no
knowledge-base entries. The exact-name rule applied below comes from
`comprehensive §2-3-1-2` (`node tools/kb/query.mjs rules "card name in brackets"`).

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate route Lv.3 [Argomon] Cost 2 | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` | "uses the alternate Argomon route for cost 2, keeps the source stack, draws and plays Rhythm"; identity assert in the catalog test |
| Printed Green Lv.3 route Cost 3 | catalog `evoCosts` | "also digivolves by the printed Lv.3 Green route for cost 3" |
| Illegal source refused | both routes | "refuses an illegal source that matches neither the printed nor the alternate route" |
| [When Digivolving] free Rhythm play | `WhenDigivolving` -> `PlayWithoutCost` from `hand`, `payCost: false`, `optional: true` | "uses the alternate Argomon route ..." |
| "If you don't have [Rhythm]" gate | `condition.kind: "youHaveNone"` (no `zone`, so the battle area, breeding excluded) | "does not play a second Rhythm when a Rhythm is already in play" |
| Inherited [On Deletion] Gain 1 memory | `OnDeletion` -> `GainMemory 1`, `isInherited: true` | "gains inherited memory when its host is deleted" |

Behavioural endpoints asserted: memory before and after (2 -> 0 alternate,
3 -> 0 printed, 5 -> 5 on the refusals), the host permanent id preserved across
digivolve, `stack` holding only the Lv.3 base, the digivolve bonus draw landing
in hand, the deck remainder, the Rhythm instance leaving hand and arriving on the
battle area with an empty stack, and battle-area size.

#### Defect found and fixed

`nameOrTrait` used `match: "name"`, which is a SUBSTRING match
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:353`). A printed
`[Rhythm]` reference is an EXACT name (comprehensive §2-3-1-2). Changed both the
target filter and the `youHaveNone` condition filter to `match: "nameExact"`.

No card other than "Rhythm" currently contains "Rhythm" in its name, so this is
correctness-only for this card today; the same defect is observable on BT17-046.

#### Remaining gaps

### BT17-048 — Argomon

Lv.5 Green/Purple Mutant Digimon, DP 7000, play cost 8. Normal evo: Green/Purple
Lv.4 for 5. Printed alternate: `[Digivolve]Lv.4 [Argomon]: Cost 4`.

#### Printed clauses

1. `[Digivolve]Lv.4 [Argomon]: Cost 4` — alternate evolution route.
2. When digivolving into this card, by suspending up to 5 Tamers, reduce the
   digivolution cost by 1 per Tamer suspended.
3. `[Opponent's Turn]` None of your opponent's Tamers can unsuspend.
4. `[On Deletion]` If 4+ [Argomon] in your trash, you may play 1 level 6
   [Argomon] from hand without paying the cost.
5. Inherited `[When Attacking][Once Per Turn]` By suspending 1 [Rhythm],
   unsuspend this Digimon.

#### Q&A covered (KB: Q2799, Q2800, Q2808)

- Q2799 (choose Tamers from either player for the suspend cost): behavioural —
  the cost filter is `{ kind: ["Tamer"] }` with no `controller`, so both players'
  Tamers are eligible. Test `Q2799 suspends an opposing Tamer...` digivolves with
  only an opposing Tamer available and confirms it is suspended and the cost drops
  by 1 (memory 8 -> 4). Test `reduces its own digivolution cost...` covers the
  own-Tamer side.
- Q2800 (deleted card counts itself toward the 4+ threshold): behavioural —
  test `with 3 Argomon in trash before deletion...` deletes the on-field Argomon
  (3 pre-existing + itself = 4) and confirms the Lv.6 Argomon (BT17-051) enters
  play from hand.
- Q2808 (Argomon removed from trash before the triggered [On Deletion] activates
  cannot activate): untestable at this layer — the engine does not model the
  trash-membership re-check between trigger and activation. Documented, no test.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity + 3 routes | `matches the catalog identity...` | catalog + `digivolutionRequirement`, Static Replacement |
| Route exactness (namesExact) | `honors the alternate cost-4 route only from an exact [Argomon] base` | `digivolutionRequirement[0].namesExact` |
| Cost reduction per Tamer | `reduces its own digivolution cost once per suspended Tamer` | Static `Replacement wouldDigivolve reduceCost` |
| Opponent Tamer lock | `keeps opposing Tamers suspended through the opponent's unsuspend phase...` + structural | `OpponentsTurn Restrict unsuspend` |
| On-deletion play Lv.6 | `with 3 Argomon in trash before deletion...` | `OnDeletion PlayWithoutCost` |
| Inherited unsuspend | `naturally unsuspends its host after attacking...` | inherited `WhenAttacking Unsuspend`, `frequency OncePerTurn` |
| Once-per-turn + reset | `inherited unsuspend is once per turn and resets on the next own turn` | inherited `frequency OncePerTurn` |
| Cost targets opposing Tamer | `Q2799 suspends an opposing Tamer...` | Static `Replacement` cost filter `{ kind: ["Tamer"] }` |

#### IR change (report to coordinator)

Changed `digivolutionRequirement[0]` from `names: ["Argomon"]` to
`namesExact: ["Argomon"]`. The printed route carries no "in name", so per the
coordinator route decision it must be exact-name (`cardData.ts` nameExact gate,
line 488). Behavioural proof: a base named exactly `Argomon` (BT17-045) takes the
cost-4 route (memory 8 -> 4); a near-name base `Gargomon` (BT17-046, Lv.4
Green/Purple, name contains "argomon") is refused on the alternate route and
falls back to the catalog Lv.4 route at cost 5 (memory 8 -> 3). This doubles as
the required illegal-source / near-name negative.

A true case-sensitive substring near-name (a base named with capital `Argomon`
as a proper substring) does not exist in the catalog, so `names` vs `namesExact`
are otherwise behaviourally identical here; `Gargomon` differs only in case.

Consequence: `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` still snapshots
`names: ["Argomon"]`, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` goes red until the
coordinator regenerates the set snapshot. effects.json not touched (not an
allowed file).

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` — clean.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` / `oxfmt --check` on both files — clean.

#### Defects fixed locally

- Route exactness: `names` -> `namesExact` (above), with new behavioural gating test.
- Added behavioural proof for the `[Opponent's Turn]` Tamer-unsuspend lock
  (previously structural-only): opposing Tamer stays suspended through
  `unsuspendForActivePhase(1)` while an opposing Digimon control unsuspends,
  confirming the Tamer-only scope. Reuses the BT11-066 / BT2-049 pattern.
- Added the alternate cost-4 route memory-delta test (previously the only evolve
  test defaulted to the normal cost-5 route and never exercised `useAlternateCost`).

#### Remaining gaps

- The single-Rhythm inherited test (`naturally unsuspends...`) proves the host
  ends unsuspended (uniquely the inherited effect's doing, since attacking is the
  only thing that suspended it), but its `rhythm.isSuspended` assertion is
  confounded by Rhythm's own reactive self-suspend. The dedicated once-per-turn
  test avoids that confound by asserting host suspension state and Rhythm-suspend
  counts across two Rhythms, so the confound is no longer material to the axis.
- No engine seams hit; no `it.fails` retained.

#### Follow-up note (behavioural axis lift to 2)

Added two behavioural tests at the coordinator's request:

- `inherited unsuspend is once per turn and resets on the next own turn`: through
  the real turn loop (`runOneTurn` / `advance().runTurn`). Attack 1 fires the
  effect (host unsuspended, one of two Rhythms suspended); a second attack the
  same turn leaves the host suspended even though the spare Rhythm is still
  unsuspended (refusal, not cost exhaustion); after a real opponent turn the host
  is readied by the unsuspend phase and a third attack fires the effect again.
  `runOneTurn` runs one turn and stops at End without handing over, so the test
  emulates the production `run()` loop's `passTurn` (flip seat, negate the gauge)
  between turns.
- `Q2799 suspends an opposing Tamer...`: digivolves with only an opposing Tamer
  present and asserts it is suspended for the cost, reducing memory 8 -> 4.
  Reachable — no seam.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Catalog identity, colors, evoCosts, DP and effect text all match.

- peer/stack: 2 — real evolution stack (BT17-045 -> BT17-048), real inherited-in-stack case (BT17-051 host over BT17-048), plus Argomon-vs-Gargomon and Tamer-vs-Digimon comparatives.
- delivery gates: 0.

### BT17-050 — Parasitemon

#### Card summary (printed clauses)

Digimon, Lv6, Green, DP 4000, play 9, evo {Green, Lv5, memory 3}. Types: Parasite.

1. `[Hand] [Main]` By paying 4 cost **and placing this card as 1 of your level 5+ Digimon's bottom digivolution card**, you may suspend 1 of your opponent's Digimon and attack an opponent's Digimon with the Digimon this was placed under.
2. `＜Alliance＞`
3. `[End of Attack]` You may place this Digimon as the bottom digivolution card of 1 of your other Digimon.

Inherited:
4. `[All Turns]` When an opponent's effect would delete this Digimon, you may play 1 [Parasitemon] from this Digimon's digivolution cards without paying the cost.
5. `[Your Turn]` This Digimon gets +3000 DP.

#### Q&A ids covered

- **Q2803** (cannot activate with no Lv5+ host): retained red as `it.fails` — engine seam, see defects.
- **Q2804** (place, then legally do neither suspend nor attack): retained red as `it.fails` — engine seam.
- **Q2805** (multiple buried [Parasitemon] each play themselves on host deletion): green, seam-driven (opponent effect-resolution window). Both copies play.

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
|---|---|---|
| Identity + printed text | "matches the catalog identity…" | catalog |
| Modal shape, EndOfAttack, both inherited, Replacement | "carries the modal…" | `Main` Modal / `EndOfAttack` / `AllTurns` / `YourTurn` |
| Clause 1 place(bottom)+suspend+attack | "naturally pays 4 from hand…" | `Main` Modal option[0] |
| Clause 1 activation gate (Q2803) | `it.fails` "Q2803…" | seam |
| Clause 1 optional bundle (Q2804) | `it.fails` "Q2804…" | seam |
| Clause 3 relocate + own-source shed + confers +3000 | "[End of Attack]…relocates as the bottom card…" | `EndOfAttack` PlaceUnder |
| Clause 4 + Q2805 | "Q2805: an opponent effect…" | `AllTurns` Replacement → PlayWithoutCost |
| Clause 5 +3000 (comparative: bare vs boosted host) | "[Your Turn] inherited…" | `YourTurn` ModifyDP |
| ＜Alliance＞ | exercised (decline path) in relocate test; keyword itself is engine-owned | `Static` keyword |

#### Defects fixed locally (module IR — `BT17-050.ts`)

1. **Main placement landed at the top, not the bottom.** Printed "bottom digivolution card". With an existing source under the host, Parasitemon was inserted directly beneath the top card (index 1) instead of the bottom (index 0). Added `position: "bottom"` to the `Main` option[0] `PlaceUnder`. (`targetIsPermanent: true` was tried and reverted — it broke `bindHostAs`, so the host never attacked.)
2. **`[End of Attack]` relocate did nothing.** The `EndOfAttack` `PlaceUnder` lacked `targetIsPermanent`, `shedOwnCards`, and `position: "bottom"` that its twin EX2-028 carries. Without them the relocate never resolved. Added all three; the relocate now places Parasitemon as the bottom card of another Digimon and trashes its own digivolution source, matching EX2-028.

Net module diff: +4 lines. `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` unchanged and honest. Exactly one `registerIrCard`.

#### Retained red (engine seams — `it.fails`, do not weaken)

1. **Q2803 — placement is not an activation gate.** File/function: `interpreter/actions/modal.ts` `canAttemptModalAction` + the `payMemory` cost path. The Lv5+ placement is modeled as `Main` option[0]'s first action, not part of `cost`. `canAttemptModalAction` treats any non-`RawUnparsed` `PlaceUnder` as attemptable, so with no Lv5+ Digimon the modal is still offered, the 4 memory is charged, `PlaceUnder` then finds no host and places nothing. Expected: activation refused, memory untouched, card in hand. Actual: `applyIntent` → `{ok:true}`, memory 4→0, card stuck in hand, nothing placed. Fixing needs the placement to be a cost, which is an engine change — not done.
2. **Q2804 — one optional prompt covers cost+effect.** File/function: `interpreter/actions/runAction.ts` optional gate + `modal.ts availableOptionIndices`. The "you may suspend and attack" bundle should be independently declinable after the card is placed (Q2804 allows place-then-neither). The IR encodes the alternative as `Modal` option[1] = `[]`, but `option.some(...)` on an empty array is false so the empty branch is filtered out and never offered; and the effect's single optional prompt gates the whole cost+effect, so declining also skips the placement. There is no path that places and then declines the suspend/attack. Expected: place + paid, opponent neither suspended nor attacked. Not reachable.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q2805 proof is seam-driven (`advance.verb.deletePermanent` inside an opponent `enterEffectResolution` window) because no public intent in the fixture deletes the host by the opponent's effect. It exercises the real replacement wiring but earns structural, not natural-flow, credit.
- ＜Alliance＞ is only exercised via its decline path; the keyword's combat behavior is engine-owned and covered by AD1-009 etc.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Catalog text, colors, level, costs, and types all match the official card.

### BT17-051 — Argomon

#### Card summary

Argomon, Digimon, Lv.6, Green/Purple, Mutant, 13000 DP.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve]Lv.5 [Argomon]: Cost 4` — reduced alternate digivolution route from an
   exact-name Lv.5 [Argomon] source.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` — you may place up to 4 level-5-or-lower [Argomon] from
   trash as this Digimon's bottom digivolution cards; then delete opponent Digimon whose
   levels add up to 4 or less; for every 2 [Argomon] in this Digimon's digivolution cards,
   add 1 to the maximum level you can choose.
3. `[All Turns]` — +1000 DP for every 2 [Argomon] in its digivolution cards.
4. `[Opponent's Turn]` — none of your opponent's Tamers can unsuspend.

Inherited: none.

#### Q&A coverage

- **Q2806** ("up to 4" allows placing fewer): covered behaviourally — with only 2 Argomon in
  trash the effect places exactly 2 (stack length 2, DP 14000), proving `count:4, upTo:true`
  does not force 4. IR structural test also asserts `upTo:true`.
- **Q2807** (Lv.- Digimon cannot be chosen by the level budget): covered behaviourally — an
  opponent Lv.- Digimon (BT19-077 Calumon) survives while the eligible Lv.3 is deleted. Backed
  by the `hasLevel: true` filter on `DeleteLevelBudget`.
- **Q2808** (a source removed from trash before a triggered [On Deletion] resolves): documented
  as untestable in this lane. It requires a specific BT17-048 [On Deletion] → play-BT17-051 →
  BT17-051 [On Play] removing the [Argomon] from trash mid-chain, i.e. cross-card trigger
  ordering that this single-card fixture cannot stage without the coordinator range harness.
  The relevant guard (a card leaving trash before its [On Deletion] activates) is a general
  engine timing rule, not BT17-051 behaviour.

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 route (exact name, cost 4) | "digivolves through the printed exact-name [Argomon] route for 4…" (memory 10→6, bonus draw, stack identity) + "refuses the cost-4 route from a non-Argomon Lv.5 source…" (memory 10→5 fallback) | `digivolutionRequirement:[{level:5,namesExact:["Argomon"],cost:4,isAlternate:true}]` |
| 2 place + scaled delete | "naturally places Argomon sources before applying the scaled level budget on play" (OnPlay) + the digivolve test (WhenDigivolving) + Q2806 + Q2807 | `PlaceUnder` + `DeleteLevelBudget` on both `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` |
| 3 +1000 DP per 2 | DP asserted 15000 (4–5 sources) and 14000 (2 sources) | `ModifyDP` scaling `per:2, unit:"digivolutionCards"` |
| 4 Tamers can't unsuspend | "keeps opposing Tamers suspended through the opponent's unsuspend phase, but not their Digimon" | `Restrict restriction:"unsuspend" duration:"untilOpponentTurnEnd"` |

Structural IR-shape tests (no behavioural credit) retained for the DeleteLevelBudget and
Restrict actions.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-051.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` → **9 passed**.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` → **exit 0**.
- `pnpm exec oxlint <files>` → clean; `pnpm exec oxfmt --check <files>` → clean;
  `git diff --check` → clean.

#### Defects fixed locally

- **Route name gate was substring (`names`), must be exact (`namesExact`).** The printed route
  `[Digivolve]Lv.5 [Argomon]: Cost 4` uses a bracketed name with no "in name", so per the
  coordinator route-name decision it must be `namesExact`. Changed `names:["Argomon"]` →
  `namesExact:["Argomon"]` in `BT17-051.ts`. **This is an IR change.**
  - Note: the substring-vs-exact distinction is **unobservable in-game** here. No Lv.5 Digimon
    exists whose name contains "argomon" but is not exactly "Argomon" (all `*argomon*` cards are
    literally "Argomon"; the near-name "Gargomon" exists only at Lv.3/Lv.4). A near-name
    substring negative at Lv.5 is therefore impossible; the route negative instead uses a
    non-Argomon Lv.5 source (BT1-076 MegaKabuterimon) that falls back to the catalog cost-5
    route (memory 10→5 vs the 10→6 the cost-4 alternate would give).

#### Retained reds / seams

None. No engine seam hit; all listed clauses and Q&A behave correctly.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q2808 documented untestable (cross-card [On Deletion] timing; see above).

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Catalog `evoCosts` (Green/Purple Lv.5 cost 5) are the normal route; the printed cost-4
`[Argomon]` route is the alternate requirement and is correctly modelled separately.

- The `namesExact` change means the compiled IR no longer matches `effects.json`, so
  `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red until the set sync runs. Expected; effects.json was
  not touched.

### BT17-052 — Agumon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv.3, Black, play cost 3, DP 1000, Reptile/SoC, Virus, Rookie.
EvoCosts: Black Lv.2 cost 0.

Printed clauses:

1. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When one of your [Kosuke Kisakata] is played,
   gain 1 memory and ＜Draw 1＞.`
2. Inherited: `＜Reboot＞.`

#### Q&A ids covered

None listed. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-052` returns no knowledge-base
entries. The exact-name rule applied below is `comprehensive §2-3-1-2`.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "When one of your [Kosuke Kisakata] is played" | `AllTurns` -> `SubTrigger` `whenPlayed` with `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"` | "gains memory and draws when Kosuke Kisakata is played, only once in the turn" |
| "your" (controller scope) | `controller: "mine"` | "does not trigger on the opponent's own Kosuke Kisakata" |
| `[Kosuke Kisakata]` exact name | `match: "nameExact"` | IR-shape test |
| "gain 1 memory and ＜Draw 1＞" | `GainMemory 1` + `Draw mine 1` | main behavioural test (memory 9 -> 6: cost 4 paid, 1 gained; one card leaves the deck) |
| [Once Per Turn] same-turn refusal | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` | main behavioural test (second play: memory 6 -> 2, deck unchanged) |
| [Once Per Turn] next-own-turn reset | same | "resets the once-per-turn draw on the next own turn through the public turn flow" |
| [All Turns] | trigger `AllTurns` | the opponent-turn negative runs with `turnSeat = 1` |
| Inherited ＜Reboot＞ | `Static` `isInherited` keyword `Reboot` | "grants inherited Reboot to an evolved host" |

The reset test runs the real turn loop: `startTurnLoop`, `waitForMainPhase(0)`,
play, `endPhase`, `waitForMainPhase(1)`, `endPhase`, `waitForMainPhase(0)`, play
again, then `surrender` to close the loop. No injected timing is used anywhere in
this file.

Deck length is the reset assertion rather than memory because BT16-087 carries
`[Start of Your Turn] If you have 2 or less memory, set it to 3`, which perturbs
the memory gauge across a turn boundary.

#### Defect found and fixed

`sourceFilter.nameOrTrait` used `match: "name"` (substring). A printed
`[Kosuke Kisakata]` is an exact name (§2-3-1-2). Changed to `match: "nameExact"`.
No other catalog card contains "Kosuke Kisakata" as a substring, so this is
correctness-only today.

```

  -> 1 file, 7 tests passed

pnpm exec oxfmt <files>               -> formatted
git diff --check                      -> clean
```

#### Remaining gaps

- The reset test sets `state.memory = 9` at each main phase so a cost-4 Tamer
  play does not drive the gauge to zero and hand the turn over. That is fixture
  plumbing, not injected timing.
- Only one printed Kosuke Kisakata card exists (BT16-087), so the exact-name
  behaviour has no near-name counterexample to test against; it rests on the IR
  assert plus the shared matcher.
- ＜Reboot＞ is proven as an active keyword on the host, not by observing an
  unsuspend at the opponent's unsuspend phase.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None.

#### IR changed

Yes — `match: "name"` -> `match: "nameExact"` (one occurrence).
`BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red until the coordinator runs the set sync.

#### Peer/stack proof (session 3 addition)

`BT17-052.test.ts` adds "ignores a near-miss Tamer peer and fires only for the exact
[Kosuke Kisakata]": BT26-096 "Kosuke Misono" and BT16-087 "Kosuke Kisakata" are played from
the same hand in the same turn. Misono pays its cost 3 (memory 9 -> 6) with no memory gain
and no draw; Kisakata then pays cost 4, gains 1 memory (-> 3) and draws exactly one card.

Honest caveat on the `nameExact` fix: no card in the catalog has "Kosuke Kisakata" as a
proper substring of a longer name, so `nameExact` and the substring `name` mode cannot be
told apart behaviourally for this card. The peer above is the closest available near-miss
(shared given name), and the exactness of the fix rests on the printed-bracket convention,
not on a discriminating fixture.

Combined with the existing inherited-＜Reboot＞ stack assertion on a BT17-054 host, this
earns the peer/stack column.

| 1 (floor: can't trash past level 3) | `cannot trash past a level 3 card` | `stopAtLevel: 3` |
| 2 (restrict, play cost <= 8 only) | `blocks only player attacks by the chosen cost-8-or-lower Digimon, leaving its cost-12 peer free` | `Restrict` `playCostLte: 8` |
| 2 (can't attack **players**, not Digimon) | same test — player attack rejected `illegal-target`, permanent-directed attack accepted | `restriction: "attackPlayers"` |
| 2 (duration) | Q2809 test carries the restriction across into the opponent's turn | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` (a real `EffectDurationRef`) |
| 3 (inherited, substring name, once per turn) | `inherits de-digivolution once per turn when another Diaboromon-named Digimon is played` | `SubTrigger whenPlayed`, `sourceFilter.excludeSelf`, `nameOrTrait match: "name"`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

Names check: `[Diaboromon] in its name` is a **substring** reference, so `match: "name"` is
correct here. The set-wide `[Name]` -> `nameExact` defect does **not** apply to this module —
BT17-055 has no bracketed exact name reference.

#### Behavioural endpoints asserted

- De-Digivolve: exact instance id moved to the opponent's trash, resulting `topCard.cardId`,
  `stack` length, host top card, `pendingDecision === undefined`.
- Restriction: `isRestricted` on both the chosen target and its cost-12 peer, plus real
  `attack` intents (`{ok:false, reason:"illegal-target"}` for the player, `{ok:true}` for a
  suspended Digimon) and the defender's security count.
- Level-3 floor: opponent trash empty, top card unchanged, battle area intact, while the
  second clause still resolves on the same Digimon.
- Once per turn: second Diaboromon played in the same turn leaves the target's top card
  instance id and the opponent's trash length unchanged; after a real
  `advance(s.engine).runTurn(1)` a third Diaboromon de-digivolves again down to the level-3
  floor card.

### BT17-056 — Locomon

#### Card summary

Digimon, Lv5, Black, play cost 7, 7000 DP, Ultimate / Data / Machine.
Evo cost: Black Lv4 for 3 memory.

Printed clauses:

1. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When an attack target is switched, reveal the top 3 cards of
   your deck. Place 1 [Parasitemon] or 1 level 5 or lower black Digimon card among them as
   this Digimon's bottom digivolution card. Trash the rest.`
2. `[All Turns] When one of your Digimon's effects adds to this Digimon's digivolution cards,
   this Digimon may digivolve into [GroundLocomon] in the hand without paying the cost.`
3. Inherited: `[Your Turn] While this Digimon has the [Machine] trait, it gains ＜Collision＞.`

#### Q&A ids covered

None listed in `KB-INDEX.md`; `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-056` returns no
knowledge-base entries. Nothing to cover.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog + full IR shape | `matches the catalog and the complete IR contract` | all three effects asserted whole; `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` |
| 1 (switch trigger, reveal 3, place under, trash rest) | `places a revealed black card underneath, trashes the rest, and free-digivolves after a natural target switch` | `SubTrigger whenAttackTargetSwitched` -> `RevealAdd revealCount: 3`, `to: "placeUnder"`, `underFilter: {isSelfRef: true}`, `rest: "trash"` |
| 1 (once per turn + reset) | `reveals only once per turn and resets on the next turn` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| 2 (free digivolve into [GroundLocomon] from hand) | same behavioural test — Locomon's top card becomes BT17-058 and the hand empties | `SubTrigger onAddDigivolutionCards` -> `Digivolve payCost: false, from: ["hand"], optional: true` |
| 3 (inherited Collision, [Machine] trait gate) | `grants inherited Collision to a Machine host and withholds it from a non-Machine host` | `Aura` keyword `Collision` with `while youHave { isSelfRef, match: "trait" }` |

#### Set-wide defect found and fixed

`[Parasitemon]` and `[GroundLocomon]` are printed **exact** bracketed name references but
were compiled as `match: "name"` (substring). Both changed to `match: "nameExact"` in
`apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-056.ts`. `[Machine]` is "the [Machine] trait", so
`match: "trait"` (exact) is already correct and was left alone.

`[GroundLocomon]` `nameExact` is exercised behaviourally (the free digivolve still resolves).
`[Parasitemon]` `nameExact` is only asserted structurally: the behavioural fixture's eligible
card matches through the `orFilters` (black, level <= 5) branch, and no Parasitemon printing
is needed to prove the OR.

#### Fixture defects fixed

- The old test seeded `security: ["BT1-001"]` — a Digi-Egg in security, one of the known
  set-wide fixture smells. Replaced with inert main-deck Digimon (`BT1-009` .. `BT1-011`).
- No numeric `security: <n>` form and no injected timing (`fire`, `fireSubTrigger`,
  `fireTiming`) is used anywhere in this file. The target switch is produced naturally by
  BT17-059 Diaboromon's own `[Opponent's Turn] [Once Per Turn]` switch effect during a real
  `attack` intent. `advance(...).verb.unsuspend` is used only as plumbing between two real
  attacks, and `advance(...).runTurn(0)` drives the real turn loop for the reset.

#### Behavioural endpoints asserted

- Reveal: exact instance id inside `Permanent.stack` (i.e. beneath the top card), both
  remainder instance ids in trash, deck emptied, host `topCard.cardId`, hand size,
  defender's security length, attacker instance in trash, `pendingDecision === undefined`.
- Once per turn: two switches in one turn (two separate Diaboromon, each with its own
  once-per-turn switch) leave the deck at 3 cards and the second eligible card out of the
  stack; after a real seat-0 turn a third switch reveals again and empties the deck.
- Collision: granted on a `[Machine]` host (BT17-058 GroundLocomon), withheld on an
  `[Unidentified]` host (BT17-059 Diaboromon) carrying the same inherited source.

#### Commands and results

```

  -> 1 file passed, 4 tests passed

pnpm exec oxlint <4 files>  -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <4 files>  -> clean
git diff --check  -> clean
```

None. No `it.fails` or `it.skip`.

#### Remaining gaps

- The `[Parasitemon]` branch of the OR is not exercised behaviourally (see above).
- "Bottom digivolution card" is asserted as membership in `Permanent.stack`, not as position
  index 0; `to: "placeUnder"` is the IR's bottom placement by definition.
- The reset half of the once-per-turn test re-enters the attack window by restoring the
  captured `state.phase` after `runTurn(0)`, because `applyIntent` refuses with
  `wrong-phase` otherwise. That is hand-laid plumbing around a real turn, not a real
  untap/draw sequence into an attack.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. `inheritedEffectText` carries U+00A0 before "trait" and the test asserts the exact
bytes. The `effectText` has a double space between the two `[All Turns]` clauses, which is
what the official list prints; asserted verbatim.

#### IR changed

**Yes** — `nameExact` on two filters in `BT17-056.ts`. `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red
for BT17-056 until the coordinator runs the set sync. `effects.json` was not touched.

### BT17-057 — Chaosdramon

Digimon, Lv6, Black, DP 12000, play cost 12. Types: Machine, SoC.

#### Printed clauses

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[SoC] trait: Cost 3` — reduced alternate route (standard black
   Lv.5 route is cost 4 from `evoCosts`).
2. Static: while you have a black Tamer, cards from your trash may also be placed for this
   card's DigiXros.
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` By placing 1 [Cyborg]/[SoC] trash card as this Digimon's
   bottom digivolution card, delete up to 7 play cost's total worth of the opponent's Digimon.
4. `[All Turns]` When this Digimon would leave the battle area by an opponent's effect, by
   trashing 2 [Cyborg]/[Machine]/[SoC] digivolution cards, prevent it from leaving.
5. `[DigiXros -2] [Machinedramon] x 1 Lv.5 Digimon card w/[Cyborg] trait`.

Inherited: none.

#### Q&A covered (Q2810, Q2811, Q2812)

- Q2810 (only 1 Machinedramon placed): negative test rejects a DigiXros that places two
  Machinedramon and no Lv.5 Cyborg (the second card cannot fill the distinct Cyborg slot).
- Q2811 (trash is a legal DigiXros source with a black Tamer): RETAINED RED (engine seam,
  see below). Negative (no Tamer -> trash rejected) is green but passes trivially because
  trash never unlocks on the direct playCard path.
- Q2812 (scope of "would leave"): covered by the opponent-effect deletion (trash) prevent
  case plus the leave-mode-agnostic `wouldLeavePlay` replacement IR. The other leave modes
  (bounce, to security, to breeding, placed-under) share the same replacement event and are
  not separately exercised.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

- Clause 1 -> `digivolves by the standard black Lv.5 route for cost 4` (memory 10->6),
  `digivolves by the reduced [SoC]-trait alternate route for cost 3` (memory 10->7),
  `falls back to the standard cost-4 route when the alternate is selected over a non-[SoC]
  source` (memory 10->6 proves the SoC gate), `rejects an illegal source that matches
  neither ... route` (AD1-002 Red Lv.5) -> `digivolutionRequirement:[{level:5,traits:["SoC"],
  cost:3,isAlternate:true}]`.
- Clause 2 -> Static IR shape (`GrantStatic grant:"digixrosFromTrash"`, condition youHave
  black Tamer) + retained-red behavioural test.
- Clause 3 -> `places a qualifying trash card underneath before deleting within budget on
  play`, `cannot reach a single opposing Digimon whose play cost exceeds the budget`
  (cost-8 survives), `fires the same place-and-delete effect on digivolving` ->
  `DeleteBudget budget:7 upTo:true` with `cost:{kind:"place", destination:"digivolutionStack",
  position:"bottom"}` on both OnPlay and WhenDigivolving.
- Clause 4 -> `trashes two qualifying sources from itself to prevent opponent-effect
  deletion` (BT17-072 On Play delete; the non-qualifying BT17-055 survives in the stack) ->
  `Replacement event:"wouldLeavePlay" leaveCause:"byOpponentEffect"` with a `Prevent` cost
  trashing 2 [Cyborg]/[Machine]/[SoC] digivolution cards.
- Clause 5 -> `DigiXroses by placing a Machinedramon and a Lv.5 Cyborg Digimon underneath`
  (stack = both materials; memory 20->10 confirms -2), `rejects a DigiXros that places two
  Machinedramon ...` -> corrected `digiXrosRequirement`.

#### Defect fixed locally (IR change)

The DigiXros recipe was authored as a single impossible material:
`materials:[{names:["Machinedramon"],traits:["Cyborg"]}], count:1`. No card named
Machinedramon carries the [Cyborg] trait (all are Lv.6 Machine), so the recipe could never be
satisfied, and it dropped the second slot and the level constraint. The printed header
`[DigiXros -2] [Machinedramon] x 1 Lv.5 Digimon card w/[Cyborg] trait` is two slots — compare
BT19-063 `[DigiXros -2] [SkullKnightmon] x [DeadlyAxemon]` ->
`materials:[{names:["SkullKnightmon"]},{names:["DeadlyAxemon"]}], count:2`. Corrected to:

```
materials: [{ names: ["Machinedramon"] }, { level: 5, traits: ["Cyborg"] }], count: 2
```

Behavioural test confirms both materials place and cost drops by 2 (memory 20->10).

IR changed: YES. `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` still holds the old single-slot
recipe, so `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be RED until the coordinator runs the set sync.
`effects.json` and the catalog were not edited (not allowed).

#### Retained red (engine seam)

`it.fails` — `accepts a trash card as DigiXros material while a black Tamer is in play
(Q2811)`. The direct `playCard` DigiXros validator
(`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digiXros.ts`, `validateDigiXros`/check, `trashMax` gate ~L203)
recognizes trash materials only via the hand-authored `DIGIXROS_TRASH_NAME_ALLOWANCES` table
(`packages/shared/src/effects/data.ts`) and the `wouldBePlayed AllowDigiXrosMaterialsFromTrash`
IR. It never consults the per-seat `expandDigiXrosZones` ledger that BT17-057's Static
`digixrosFromTrash` grant populates while a black Tamer is in play; that ledger is read only on
the effect-driven play path (`interpreter/actions/play.ts:645`). BT18-065 (same grant shape)
works only because it has an entry in the shared name-allowance table. Expected `{ok:true}`
with the trash Machinedramon placed underneath; actual `{ok:false, reason:"invalid-material"}`.
Fixing correctly needs either a shared-data entry for BT17-057 (shared is not editable in this
lane) or the validateDigiXros path to consult the seat ledger (engine change, not allowed).

#### Remaining gaps

- Q2811 positive is a retained red seam (above).
- Q2812 alternate leave-modes (bounce / security / breeding / placed-under) not separately
  exercised; covered by the deletion case and the mode-agnostic replacement IR.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None in the catalog text itself. The catalog `effectText` and `evoCosts` (standard black
Lv.5 cost 4 + the printed alternate) are consistent with the corrected IR. The discrepancy was
between the module IR and the printed DigiXros header (fixed).

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-057.test.ts --maxWorkers=1
  --no-file-parallelism` -> 14 passed | 1 expected fail (15).
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` -> exit 0.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` / `oxfmt --check` on both files -> clean.
- `git diff --check` -> clean.

#### Score (worker, gates 0)

- catalog/rules: 2 — verified catalog + KB (Q2810/2811/2812) + comprehensive "would leave";
  caught a real recipe defect.
- IR trace: 2 — every clause mapped; two seams traced to file/function.
- behavioural: 2 — exact-endpoint tests for every clause; one clause is an engine seam,
  retained red honestly.
- peer/stack: 2 — comparative cases (budget subset + cost-8 ceiling; qualifying vs
  non-qualifying stack cards; distinct DigiXros slots).
- delivery gates: 0.

Total: 8/10.

#### Engine lane E2 resolution (session 3) — retained red CLOSED

Two halves, both engine-side. `validateDigiXros` never read the per-seat
`expandDigiXrosZones` ledger that the effect-driven play path already consumes, so the
direct DigiXros verb was stricter than the effect path; and the ledger was empty anyway,
because BT17-057's Static was routed through `staticModifier`'s on-field base guard while
the permission is only ever read from HAND. `isDigiXrosZoneStatic` now routes that shape to
a guard-free `digiXrosZoneStatic` builder, `runGrantStatic` records an off-field grant
through `expandDigiXrosZonesForPlay` scoped to that card's own play, and `validateDigiXros`
reads the ledger with the same quota rule `play.ts` uses.

`pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-057.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
red `Tests 14 passed | 1 expected fail (15)` -> green `Tests 15 passed (15)`.
The Q2811 negative stays green, so the black-Tamer condition is still doing the gating.

Detail: `DIGIXROS-ZONE-LEDGER-MECHANISM.md`. The section above is superseded.

### BT17-059 — Diaboromon

Lane 49. Files touched: `apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-059.test.ts` (rewritten).
`BT17-059.ts` unchanged (IR was already correct). No git writes.

#### Card summary (printed clauses)

1. **[When Digivolving]** By placing 1 [Doomsday Clock] from your hand or trash as
   this Digimon's bottom digivolution card, you may play 2 [Diaboromon] Tokens
   without paying their costs (Lv.6 / White / 3000 DP Digimon tokens).
2. **[Opponent's Turn] [Once Per Turn]** When one of your opponent's Digimon
   attacks, you may switch the attack target to 1 of your Digimon with
   [Diaboromon] in its name.

No inherited effect. Stats: Black, Lv6, play 12, 12000 DP, Unidentified,
evoCost Black Lv5 memory 4.

#### Clause -> IR -> test mapping

| Printed | IR action | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [When Digivolving] "by placing 1 [Doomsday Clock]... bottom digivolution card" | `PlayToken` with `cost: {kind:"place", from:["hand","trash"], destination:"digivolutionStack", position:"bottom", host:"self"}`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline` | place-from-hand, place-from-trash, decline-all, no-clock-negative, Q2813 it.fails |
| "play 2 [Diaboromon] Tokens without paying their costs" | `PlayToken tokens:["Diaboromon"] count:2 payCost:false` | place-from-hand (2 tokens, 3000 DP, Lv6 Digimon named Diaboromon) |
| [Opponent's Turn][Once Per Turn] "switch the attack target to 1 of your Digimon with [Diaboromon] in its name" | `OpponentsTurn` / `OncePerTurn` -> `SubTrigger whenOpponentAttacks` -> `RedirectAttack` target `nameOrTrait match:"name"` (substring) | redirect test (switch + once-per-turn + Q2815) |
| Digivolve into BT17-059 (Black Lv5 route) | catalog `evoCosts` | Black-Lv5-route test + illegal-source negative |

Name matching: the redirect uses `match:"name"` (substring) — correct for the
"...in its name" form. The cost's `[Doomsday Clock]` also compiles to
`match:"name"`; this follows the accepted house convention for bracketed exact
names (see BT23-070 "Belphemon: Sleep Mode", BT23-073 "Mother Eater"). Only
BT17-100 bears the name "Doomsday Clock", so exact vs substring is unobservable
here. No IR change made.

#### Q&A coverage (Q2813, Q2814, Q2815, Q5646)

- **Q2813** (two parts):
  - "may decline the whole effect after placing" is split by the engine into a
    single opt-in. The **decline path** (decline everything -> nothing placed,
    no tokens, digivolution still resolves) is covered green.
  - The other half — **place the clock yet decline the tokens** — is **not
    reachable**. Retained as `it.fails` naming the seam (see Retained red).
- **Q2814** (2 tokens = single play timing, "when played" triggers once): handled
  at the engine level. `primitives.ts` `playToken` plays each token under the
  shared resolving-effect `windowToken`, and its own comment cites Q2814 /
  BT2-053 for the `oncePerTiming` dedupe. Not separately testable for BT17-059:
  the Diaboromon tokens carry no counting trigger, and the cited counters live on
  other cards' inherited text. Documented as engine-covered.
- **Q2815** (may redirect to an **unsuspended** named Diaboromon): covered. The
  redirect test asserts the chosen Diaboromon is unsuspended before the switch and
  that the attack lands on it.
- **Q5646** (Diaboromon played mid-attack via BT17-100 cannot then redirect the
  attack already declared): structurally satisfied and untestable via the harness.
  `SubTrigger whenOpponentAttacks` fires at attack **declaration**; a Digimon that
  enters after declaration is not subscribed for that in-flight attack, so no
  retroactive redirect is possible. No production Intent reaches "play BT17-059
  from BT17-100 during a resolving attack", so no behavioural test is added.

#### Behavioural evidence (real Intents, no injected timing)

- **Digivolve, Black Lv5 route**: `digivolve` Intent onto BT17-056. Memory 4 -> 0,
  bonus draw taken (deck card in hand), source stack `[BT17-056]` preserved under
  BT17-059, battle area length 1.
- **Illegal source negative**: `digivolve` onto BT1-009 (Red Lv3) refused; source,
  hand and memory unchanged.
- **Place clock from hand + 2 tokens**: clock placed at the **bottom** of the
  stack (`stack[0]`), base second; hand left with only the drawn card; 2
  token permanents at 3000 DP, Lv6, named Diaboromon.
- **Place clock from trash**: same outcome sourcing the clock from trash; trash no
  longer holds it.
- **Decline all**: `autoDeclineOptional` -> no clock in stack, no tokens, clock
  still in hand, digivolution still resolved.
- **No clock available**: cost unpayable -> no tokens, digivolution still resolves.
- **Redirect + once-per-turn**: real `attack` Intent (target `{kind:"player"}`)
  from the opponent's seat. First attack switches onto the unsuspended Diaboromon
  (attacker deleted, both defender Digimon survive, security untouched); the
  second same-turn attack is **not** redirected (window spent) and ends the game.

#### Peer/stack proof

- **Comparative (same fixture)**: the redirect fixture holds a non-matching
  Digimon (BT1-009 Monodramon) beside the named Diaboromon; the name filter
  excludes it and it is spared.
- **Real evolution stack**: digivolve tests build and assert a genuine
  BT17-059-over-BT17-056 stack, plus the clock inserted at the stack bottom.

#### Defects fixed locally

- Replaced the Digi-Egg fixture: BT1-001 (Lv2 egg) in the opponent's security is
  now BT1-013 (inert main-deck Digimon). No numeric `security:<n>` form used; no
  Digi-Egg in security or deck. Deck seeds use BT1-013 (inert).
- Strengthened all assertions to exact endpoints (memory, stack order, hand
  contents, token stats, security length, deletion) and added the missing
  clauses (from-trash cost, decline path, no-clock negative, illegal source,
  Q2815 unsuspended target, comparative non-match).

#### Retained red (engine seam)

`it.fails` — "can place Doomsday Clock while declining the token play (Q2813)".
- File/function: `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts`,
  the optional gate at `ctx.ask.optional(ctx, describeAction(action))` (~line 791)
  ahead of `payCost`.
- Expected (Q2813): player may pay the "by placing" cost and still decline to play
  the tokens.
- Actual: one optional prompt covers **both** cost and effect — accept places the
  clock and plays both tokens; decline places nothing. The clock-placed-without-
  tokens outcome is unreachable. Assertion left red, seam named, no engine edit.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Once-per-turn cross-turn reset** is not driven. Same-turn refusal is proven
  through real combat, but resetting on the *next* opponent turn needs two
  opponent turns with a mid-turn attack injected; `advance().runTurn` opens and
  ends Main only and cannot inject attacks, so no behavioural reset test was
  added.
- Q2814 and Q5646 documented as engine-covered / structurally untestable (above).

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Printed text (incl. the U+00A0 before "in its name" on the redirect clause),
evoCosts and stats all match the module and tests.

#### IR changed

No.

#### Engine lane E2 resolution (session 3) — retained red CLOSED

`BT17-059.ts` sets `payCostBeforeOptional: true` on the `PlayToken`, and `runActionInner`
now pays a mandatory activation cost under an optional payload before offering the payload
(Q2813). `it.fails` flipped to `it`.

`pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-059.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
red `Tests 9 passed | 1 expected fail (10)` -> green `Tests 9 passed (9)`.

The superseded test "declines the whole optional effect: no clock placed, no tokens" was

### BT17-060 — Armageddemon

Digimon, Lv7, Black/White, [Unidentified], play cost 16, DP 15000, evolution: Black Lv6
for 7 or White Lv6 for 7. No inherited effect. No knowledge-base entries
(`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-060` returns "no knowledge-base entries"), so there
are no Q&A ids to cover. BT17-060 is not the BT17 banlist entry (that is BT17-069).

#### Printed clauses

1. `When you would play this card from the hand, by placing up to 13 cards with the
   [Unidentified] trait or [Diaboromon] in its text from your trash at the bottom of your
   deck, reduce the cost by 1 for each card.`
2. `＜Rush＞.` `＜Blocker＞.` `＜Reboot＞`
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Delete any of your opponent's Digimon with a total play
   cost up to 15.`
4. `[Your Turn] This Digimon can attack your opponent's unsuspended Digimon.`

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Play-from-hand cost reduction | `deletes budget 15, gains its keywords, and attacks unsuspended` — three eligible trash cards move to the deck bottom and memory lands on 3 (16 - 3 reduction paid from 16 memory) | outer `Replacement` on `wouldBePlayed`, nested `Replacement` `mode: "reduceCost"`, `amountFromPaidCost`, `cost.kind: "return"`, `to: "deckBottom"`, `count: 13`, `upTo: true`, `scaling.per: 1` |
| "[Unidentified] trait or [Diaboromon] in its text" | `reduces hand play cost by one per eligible trash card, up to thirteen` | `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Unidentified"], match: "trait" }, { tokens: ["Diaboromon"], match: "text", orPrevious: true }]` |
| Rush / Blocker / Reboot | `has Rush, Blocker, Reboot, ...` (structural) and `deletes budget 15, ...` (`observe(...).hasKeyword` on the live permanent; the same test attacks on the turn it was played, which is Rush behaviourally, and the OPPONENT's copy opens a real block window that must be declined, which is Blocker behaviourally) | three `Static` keyword effects |
| `[On Play]` delete, budget 15 | `deletes budget 15, gains its keywords, and attacks unsuspended` — 7 + 8 = 15 deleted, the 16-cost permanent survives | `OnPlay` -> `DeleteBudget`, `budget: 15`, `upTo: true`, `filter.controller: "opponent"` |
| `[When Digivolving]` delete, budget respected | `respects the 15 budget when the opponent's Digimon cannot all fit, on [When Digivolving]` — 7 + 12 = 19 exceeds the budget, exactly one survives | `WhenDigivolving` -> same `DeleteBudget` |
| `[Your Turn]` attack unsuspended | `deletes budget 15, ...` — the target is asserted unsuspended, the attack is accepted, the block window is declined, and the resulting battle deletes both 15000 DP Digimon | `YourTurn` -> `GrantCanAttackUnsuspended`, `duration: "permanent"`, `isSelf: true` |
| Catalog agreement | `matches the catalog printed text, colours and evolution costs` | — |

#### Set-wide `[Name]` defect

Not present. `[Unidentified]` is a printed trait ("with the [Unidentified] trait") and is
compiled as `match: "trait"` — exact, which is correct. `[Diaboromon]` is printed as
"[Diaboromon] in its text" and is compiled as `match: "text"`, which is the substring form
the printed wording asks for. Nothing on this card is compiled as `match: "name"`.

#### Evidence notes

- The cost reduction is asserted by the MEMORY DELTA (16 -> 3) and by the three named
  trash instances being absent from trash and present in the deck, not by `{ ok: true }`.
- The budget clause is asserted twice, positively and negatively: at exactly 15 the two
  cheaper permanents go and the 16-cost one stays; at 19 the engine can only take a subset
  and exactly one permanent survives, with the surviving/deleted split checked against the
  catalog play costs.
- The attack test discovered a real fixture trap: the opponent's Armageddemon has
  ＜Blocker＞, so the defending seat's block window must be answered
  (`applyIntent(1, { type: "declineBlock" })`) or the attack never resolves while the
  intent still returns `{ ok: true }`. This is worth adding to the shared trap list.
- `duration: "permanent"` on `GrantCanAttackUnsuspended` is a real `EffectDurationRef`.
- Fixtures use inert main-deck Digimon, no Digi-Egg in security or deck, no numeric
  `security: <n>` form, and keep a spare card in hand.

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-060.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 5 passed (5)
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-060.test.ts        -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT17/BT17-060.test.ts -> correct format
git diff --check                                                 -> clean
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json
  -> no error in this file (remaining errors belong to other lanes: BT17-008,
     BT17-011, BT17-092)
```

#### Defects, gaps, discrepancies

- No module defect found; `BT17-060.ts` is unchanged, so the compiled IR is unchanged and
  `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` is unaffected.
- No retained red tests, no engine seam hit.
- No catalog discrepancy. The catalog carries U+00A0 before "trait" and before "in its
  text"; the test asserts those exact bytes.
- Remaining gaps:
  - The "up to 13" upper bound is only asserted structurally (`count: 13, upTo: true`);
    no fixture seeds 14 eligible trash cards to prove the cap behaviourally.
  - The declined branch of the optional reduction (`optional: true`,
    `abortOnDecline: true`) is untested — no `autoDeclineOptional` run asserts that
    declining pays the full 16 or aborts the play.
  - ＜Reboot＞ is proven only by `hasKeyword`; no test unsuspends it during the opponent's
    turn through the real turn loop.
  - Peer/stack: the [When Digivolving] test is a real evolution-stack case (Lv6 black
    source, cost 7, memory 7 -> 0), and both delete tests are comparative across several
    opponent permanents, but no near-matching peer card is placed alongside the cost
    reduction to prove the trash filter rejects an ineligible card.

#### Peer/stack proof (session 3 addition)

`BT17-060.test.ts` adds "counts only the eligible trash cards and leaves an ineligible peer
behind", which puts an ineligible peer in the cost-reduction fixture for the first time:

- BT17-053 Keramon matches by the `[Unidentified]` trait,
- BT17-100 Doomsday Clock matches only by `[Diaboromon]` in its text (it is an Option with
  no Unidentified trait), and
- BT1-009 Monodramon matches neither.

Playing Armageddemon from hand with memory 16 leaves memory 2 — exactly two reductions —
with both eligible cards at the bottom of the deck and Monodramon still the only card in the
trash. This exercises both branches of the `orPrevious` name/trait/text filter and its
negative in one fixture, beside the existing real Lv6 -> Lv7 evolution stack case.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] By deleting 1 of your other Digimon, delete 1 of your opponent's level 4 or lower Digimon.`
   — one optional cost clause (delete an own *other* Digimon) gating one deletion clause
   (opponent, level 4 or lower).
2. Inherited: `＜Retaliation＞.`

#### Q&A ids covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-061` returns `(no knowledge-base entries)`. The lane
assignment lists no Q&A ids. Nothing to cover.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity + whole IR | `matches the catalog and the complete IR contract` | `compiled.effects` deep equality, `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` |
| Cost unpayable -> no deletion | `does not resolve the deletion when no other own Digimon can pay the cost` | `cost.kind: "deleteOwn"` + `abortOnDecline: true` |
| Cost paid -> opposing Lv4 deleted, Lv5 untouched | `deletes another own Digimon to delete only the level-4 opponent` | `levelComparison: { op: "lte", value: 4 }` |
| Optional refusal keeps both boards | `keeps both Digimon when the optional cost is declined` | `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` |
| Digivolution requirements | `digivolves from a level 2 Purple/Green source in breeding for 0 memory` (`it.each`) | catalog `evoCosts` |
| Illegal source | `refuses a level 2 Red source that matches neither digivolution requirement` | — |
| Inherited ＜Retaliation＞ | `grants inherited Retaliation and deletes the winning battle opponent` | `isInherited: true`, `keywords: [Retaliation]` |

Endpoints asserted after `settle`: battle-area permanent ids per seat, trash card ids per
seat, hand card ids, deck length, `state.memory`, `state.pendingDecision === undefined`,
breeding permanent identity and stack contents.

Fixtures use inert main-deck Digimon only (`BT1-009`, `BT1-010`, `BT4-025`, `BT17-025`);
no Digi-Egg is seeded into security or deck, and no numeric `security: <n>` form appears.
The breeding tests use real Digi-Eggs in the breeding area, which is their legal zone.

#### Defects found and fixed locally

The pre-existing suite proved the IR shape with two `toMatchObject` assertions and had no
coverage for the declined branch, no evolution proof and no exact endpoints (it asserted

  deep equality;
- added the `autoDeclineOptional` branch;
- added both printed digivolution routes and an illegal-source negative;
- tightened every endpoint to exact zone contents, memory and no pending decision.

None.

  hand gains the deck card). That matches the general digivolve draw and no ruling in the

  verify.
- ＜Retaliation＞ is proven through one battle the host loses. The keyword's interaction
  with a battle the host wins, or with a removal-by-effect, is engine-level and out of

`BT17-061.test.ts` adds "builds a real stack from a played Goblimon and grants Retaliation
once buried", replacing reliance on a pre-seeded `under:` fixture for the inherited keyword:

opponent survives beside the deleted level-4), this closes the peer/stack column.

### BT17-062 — Dorumon

#### Card

Digimon, Lv3, Purple/Black, DP 2000, play cost 3, types Beast / X Antibody / SoC.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve][Dorimon]: Cost 0` — alternate, name-based digivolution route.
2. `[When Attacking]` If `[Kosuke Kisakata]` is in this Digimon's digivolution cards
   **and** the opponent has a level 6 or higher Digimon, this Digimon **may**
   digivolve into `[Dorugoramon]` in the hand for a digivolution cost of 4,
   ignoring digivolution requirements.
3. Inherited: `＜Reboot＞.`

Q&A ids to cover: none. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT17-062` returns
"no knowledge-base entries", so there is nothing to cover or excuse.

#### Defect found and fixed

The compiled IR used `match: "name"` (substring) for both printed exact
references, `[Kosuke Kisakata]` and `[Dorugoramon]`. This is the known set-wide
defect. Consequence for this card was real, not cosmetic: `Dorugoramon` as a
substring also matches **DexDorugoramon** (BT17-073, BT9-081), an illegal target
for a printed `[Dorugoramon]` reference. The previous behavioural test actually
digivolved into BT17-073 and passed, i.e. it asserted the wrong rule.

Fix: both tokens now compile as `match: "nameExact"`. The behavioural test uses a
real Dorugoramon (BT7-065) and a new negative proves DexDorugoramon is refused.

IR changed: **yes** — `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red until the
coordinator runs the set sync. `effects.json` was not touched.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, colors, evoCosts, inherited text | "matches the catalog printed text…" | catalog |
| `[Digivolve][Dorimon]: Cost 0` shape | "compiles the printed [Digivolve][Dorimon]…" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| `[Dorimon]` route pays 0 + bonus draw | "digivolves from a Dorimon egg for the printed cost of 0…" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Catalog Black Lv2 route pays 1 (comparative) | "costs 1 on the catalog Black route…" | catalog `evoCosts` |
| Illegal source refused on both routes | "refuses both routes from an off-color egg…" | both routes |
| `[When Attacking]` condition + cost 4 + ignore reqs | "requires Kosuke underneath and an opposing level-6…" | `effects[0].actions[0]` |
| Full positive: both conditions met, cost 4 paid, stack identity | "digivolves into Dorugoramon for 4 while attacking…" | `effects[0].actions[0]` |
| `[Dorugoramon]` is exact, not substring | "does not accept DexDorugoramon…" | `into.nameOrTrait[0].match` |
| Negative: no opposing Lv6 | "does not offer the attack evolution without an opposing level 6" | `opponentHas` condition |
| Negative: no Kosuke underneath | "does not offer the attack evolution without Kosuke…" | `selfDigivolutionStackHasTrait` |
| Inherited `＜Reboot＞` | IR test + "grants inherited Reboot to its host" | `effects[1]` |

Endpoints asserted throughout: memory before/after (4 -> 0 on the paid chain,
unchanged on every refusal), `Permanent.stack` card ids in order, top-card
instance id, hand instance ids, deck length, breeding permanent identity.

#### Fixture hygiene

- No Digi-Egg in security or deck; the numeric `security: <n>` form is gone
  (replaced with an explicit inert `BT1-009`).
- No injected timing: every case runs through `applyIntent` + `settle`.
- Hosts that must survive a check use `dp: 20_000`.
- The Dorimon egg (BT16-005) is seeded in breeding, where a Digi-Egg belongs.

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT17/BT17-062.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> 1 file passed, 12 tests passed
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json  -> no error in BT17-062.*
pnpm exec oxlint <files> -> clean; pnpm exec oxfmt --check <files> -> clean; git diff --check -> clean
```

The api-package `tsc` run reports pre-existing errors in other lanes' files
(BT17-055.test.ts, BT17-058.test.ts). None are mine.

#### Remaining gaps

- No banlist/Q&A record exists for this card, so nothing is claimed there.
- The attack chain is proven with the opponent's Digimon at level 6 exactly and
  at level 5; a level 7+ case would add nothing the `gte` comparison does not
  already cover.

#### Catalog discrepancies

None. Catalog text matches the assignment text byte for byte.

references `[HippoGryphonmon]` and `[Murmukusmon]` — the known set-wide defect.

### BT17-064 — Pipismon

Lane 19. Digimon, Lv4, Purple/Blue, play cost 5, DP 5000, Armor Form, Free, Mutant.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|---|
| C1 | `[Digivolve][Patamon]: Cost 2` — alternate route from an exactly named Patamon source |
| C2 | `＜Armor Purge＞` — keyword label only, no printed rules text on this card |
| C3 | `[When Digivolving] trash the bottom 2 digivolution cards of 1 of your opponent's Digimon.` |
| C4 | `[Your Turn] When this Digimon attacks an opponent's Digimon with no digivolution cards, delete that Digimon.` |

No inherited effect. Catalog `effectText` matches the official list; no discrepancy found.

#### Q&A coverage

| Id | Covered by | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Q2816 | `Q2816: an inherited mid-attack source trash does not retroactively arm the delete` | retained RED as `it.fails` — engine gap, see below |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | `requires the exact printed [Patamon] name on the digivolution source`; `uses the Patamon evolution route and trashes exactly two bottom sources`; `refuses the [Patamon] route from a source that is not named Patamon` | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| C2 | `trashes the bottom two digivolution cards of one opposing Digimon` (structural) | `Static` effect `keywords[0]` |
| C3 | `uses the Patamon evolution route and trashes exactly two bottom sources` | `WhenDigivolving` → `TrashDigivolution { amount: 2, fromTop: false }` |
| C4 | `deletes a no-source combat target before battle`; `does not trigger if the target had a source when the attack was declared`; Q2816 test | `YourTurn` → `SubTrigger whenAttacking` → `Delete` gated by `attackTargetMatchesFilter` |

Behavioural endpoints asserted: stack contents after the trash (`["BT17-025"]` left, `BT1-010`/`BT1-011` in the opponent's trash), memory 2 → 0 for the alternate route, the evolving stack identity (`topCard` BT17-064 over `BT16-016`), hand size and memory unchanged on the illegal route, and battle-area membership by `permanentId` on both delete cases. No injected timing anywhere in this file: every attack goes through `applyIntent`.

#### Defect fixed locally

Set-wide `[Name]` defect present. `BT17-064.ts` compiled the printed exact `[Patamon]` as
`names: ["Patamon"]`, the SUBSTRING field
(`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/requirements/digivolve.ts`, matched with `name.includes(n)` at
`apps/api/src/engine/cards/cardData.ts:483`). Changed to `namesExact: ["Patamon"]`.

Latent, not currently exploitable: the only other catalog name containing "Patamon" is
`P-023 Patamon's Confession`, an Option, which can never be a digivolution source. So no
behavioural negative can distinguish the two fields today; the IR shape is asserted instead.

**IR changed: yes.** `BT17-catalog-sync.test.ts` will be red until the coordinator re-syncs
`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json`, which still holds `names`. Runtime legality reads
`compiledEffects` (effects.json) through `digivolutionRequirementsFor`, so the fix is inert until
that sync lands.

#### Retained red

`it.fails("Q2816: ...")`. Seam: `attackTargetMatchesFilter` in
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/conditions.ts:58`. It resolves the declared defender via
`ctx.game.permanentById` and re-runs `candidatePermanents` against LIVE state at the moment the
sub-trigger fires. The trigger payload carries no declaration-time snapshot of the defender's
digivolution cards.

- Setup: BT17-064 (DP 5000) over BT16-016 [Patamon]; defender BT17-025 (DP 9000) with one
  digivolution card. BT16-016's inherited `[When Attacking]` trashes the defender's only source in
  the same window.
- Expected (Q2816): the clause does not trigger; the defender keeps its slot and BT17-064 loses the
  battle.
- Actual: the defender is deleted before damage (`BT17-025` in trash, BT17-064 alive).

Not fixable from card IR: a `condition` on the `SubTrigger` action itself is evaluated while the
watcher is installed, when the payload has no defender at all. Engine change required; not made,
per the brief.

#### Remaining gaps

- C2 `＜Armor Purge＞` carries no printed rules text on this card, so only its presence in the IR is
  asserted.
- C4's `[Your Turn]` turn gate is not exercised on the opponent's turn: this Digimon cannot attack
  then, so the gate has no observable opponent-turn behaviour.

| Peer/stack proof | 2 | Real evolution stack (BT16-016 under BT17-064, stack identity asserted) and a comparative same-fixture case: two opposing Digimon where only the source-less one is deleted |
